> **🎚️ MODEL SAMPLING SETTINGS v1 SHIPPED — AND IT FIXES A LIVE TRUNCATION BUG (2026-08-20, Opus-5 builder to the Fable spec over `docs/research/2026-08-20-model-settings.md`; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** the harness sent **ZERO sampling parameters, anywhere** — every generation ran on whatever the engine's own defaults were, and those differ wildly per engine. **THE BUG THAT MADE THIS URGENT: `mlx_lm.server` defaults `max_tokens` to 512 (and `mlx_vlm` to 2048) when the request body OMITS it, and our body omitted it — so every reply from an MLX text model was being silently cut at 512 tokens.** llama.cpp's `-n` default is `-1` (unbounded), which is exactly why the gguf lane never showed the symptom and nobody found it. **The direct lane now ALWAYS sends an explicit `max_tokens` (default 4096), unconditionally, for every model, whether or not it has any settings saved** — that part is a bug fix, not a feature, and it is pinned by a test that walks every engine plus the unknown/None/junk-entry cases. **STORAGE:** one optional `settings` dict per chat-model registry entry (`{temperature, top_p, top_k, min_p, repeat_penalty, repeat_last_n, max_tokens, seed, stop?}`), written through the EXISTING atomic `_registry_update` whose None-removes-key grammar is exactly right for "reset" (a reset is the ABSENCE of a key, never a stored null — the convention the voice pins established). **`settings` is added to `seed_registry.USER_KEYS`** — without that one word a single **RESCAN** click would have silently reset every tuned model, which is the *same defect class* that un-pinned every voice once already; a rescan-merge test now proves a `local` AND an `lmstudio-import` entry keep their settings (and that a fresh scan can never invent the key). **ENGINE AWARENESS IS THE INTERESTING PART, and it is asserted in BOTH directions:** the MLX servers name the two penalty fields **`repetition_penalty` / `repetition_context_size`**, so a body carrying llama.cpp's `repeat_penalty` / `repeat_last_n` would be **silently ignored** there — the merge translates per engine (`SAMPLING_WIRE`, engine derived exactly as `start_component.sh:64-66` does: format mlx + vision → mlx-vlm, mlx → mlx-lm, else llama.cpp) and the tests assert the right name is present AND the wrong one is absent. The numeric meaning is identical on both scales (logits of repeated tokens divided by the value, 1.0 = no-op); only the *disabled sentinel* differs (llama.cpp 1.0, MLX 0.0), so **`repeat_penalty`'s allowed floor is pinned at 1.0** and MLX's 0.0 is structurally unreachable — a value can never mean two different things to the two engines. **`seed` is view-only until set:** -1 means "random" to llama.cpp but is not a documented MLX sentinel, so anything < 0 is simply **not put on the wire**. **DEFAULTS = "harness defaults", deliberately not "recommended":** temp **0.7** (llama.cpp's own 0.8 is creative-completion tuned; 0.7 is the conventional assistant setting and is gentler on heavy quants), top_p 0.95, top_k 40, min_p 0.05, **repeat 1.1 / 256**, max_tokens 4096, seed -1. ⚠️ **Only 1.1/256 has evidence behind it** (the 2026-08-06 gemma-31B `C-C-C-…` loop incident); every other number is convention, and the UI says "harness defaults" for exactly that reason. **THE BURIED CONSTANT IS NOW VISIBLE:** `--repeat-penalty 1.1 --repeat-last-n 256` **stays on the llama-server launch line unchanged** (Fable D2: argv is the engine-default FLOOR reaching the surfaces we do not own — the Agent and Hermes lanes build their own request bodies and never send a penalty; a request body WINS over argv), but the same two numbers are now the visible, editable defaults in the panel, and `start_component.sh` gained a comment pointing at both the default table and the research doc so the constant stops being mysterious. **LANE HONESTY (Fable D1), said on the group rather than implied:** *"Applies to CHAT (direct) turns — the Agent and Hermes lanes build their own requests and use their own engines' settings. Values apply on your next message; no model reload."* There is **no Odysseus or Hermes fan-out**, and that is a decision, not an omission: Odysseus sends only `temperature` from one **global, admin-only** preset row (writing ours there would retune every Odysseus chat, including ones on a different model) and **Hermes has no `model.temperature` key at all** — writing one would be a silent no-op. **D3: v1 IS SAMPLING ONLY — the Load group (ctx / KV-quant / flash-attn / gpu-layers) was NOT built**, because every one of those costs a 60-90s model reload and needs its own explicit Reload affordance. **SEAM (⚠️ tagged): there is deliberately no GET route.** `/api/models` already carries every installed entry and the panel already polls it, so the read side is two extra keys on that payload (`settings` = the raw pin, `sampling` = the rendered view) and the write side is ONE new `POST /api/models/settings {id, settings:{key: value|null}}` (+`{id, reset:true}`). The endpoint validates against a per-engine range table and **400s** on an unknown model, a voice model, a field this engine cannot honour, or an out-of-range value — a slider can never post `top_p: 5` and break a turn. **PANEL:** a collapsed **`▸ Sampling · N changed`** group in the Models detail pane under the actions row, one compact `.cap-row` per field with the value box, the default shown inline (`default 0.7 — running the default`), a per-field **`↺`** that only appears when overridden, an empty box = reset, and a **`Reset all to harness defaults`** chip. **The panel invents NO field list of its own** — it renders `m.sampling.fields` computed by the bridge, so labels are the real wire names and a field the engine cannot honour is **ABSENT rather than greyed out** (LM Studio's hide-not-grey pattern; a control that cannot work must not be drawn). **ZERO new CSS — 520/520 balanced, no rule added** (the whole group is `.cap-card`/`.cap-row`/`.cap-name`/`.cap-desc`/`.cap-ctl`/`.cap-inp`/`.cap-btn`/`.cap-count` from the Capabilities grammar; the one error line uses an inline style rather than a new class because `.cap-err` is scoped to `.cap-row`). Two small correctness details worth knowing: the model id is **never interpolated into an onclick** (the section only ever draws the SELECTED model, so handlers read `mSel.id` — a model id with an apostrophe cannot break out), and a background re-render of the Models pane (a finishing download, a voice-default change) **cannot wipe a half-typed value** (`renderModels` skips `renderDetail` while focus is inside the group; an explicit selection still re-renders unconditionally). **VALIDATION: 29 py + 18 js suites green, contract 105, ast OK, `bash -n` clean on start_component.sh, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean, CSS 520/520 with ZERO new rules.** New `bridge/tests/test_model_settings.py` — the full merge decision table (defaults→override→engine translation, both wire directions, the seed rule, boundary-inclusive ranges, an 18-case junk-totality table where every surprise type/range falls back to the default and a good key survives beside a bad one), the always-send-`max_tokens` invariant across all engines, `sampling_view` (labels ARE the wire names, changed-count, junk override not shown as changed, view/merge field sets can never disagree), the **USER_KEYS rescan-preservation** proof, and wiring greps for the direct lane / the read side / the endpoint / the argv floor / the panel. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) the read side folded into `/api/models` instead of a GET route (smaller seam, but `/api/models` grew two keys and now runs `sampling_view` per model per poll — pure, cheap, but it is work on a polled path); (b) **`temperature` 0.7 as the default changes today's behaviour on BOTH lanes' engines** — llama.cpp was effectively running 0.8 and MLX was running **0.0 (greedy)**, so MLX replies will become less deterministic than they were, by design (0.0 greedy is a surprising chat default and made the two engines different products), but it IS a behaviour change nobody asked for explicitly; (c) `repeat_penalty`'s floor pinned at 1.0 means the penalty **cannot be turned off** from this UI (1.0 is a no-op, which is the same thing, but it is not spelled "off"); (d) `max_tokens` cannot be set to -1/unbounded (range starts at 1) — deliberate, because unbounded is a footgun on MLX, but it does remove a capability llama.cpp has; (e) **`stop` is supported in storage + merge but has NO UI row in v1** (a comma-separated box makes a comma-in-a-stop-string ambiguous) — a hand-edited registry works, the panel just cannot reach it; (f) `seed` gets a UI row even though -1 is never sent, so the row reads "default -1" and does nothing until you type a number; (g) an unknown model (not in the registry) falls back to the **llamacpp** key names — harmless, since both MLX servers ignore unknown body fields, but it is a fallback not a certainty; (h) group state (`samplingOpen`) is module-level session state, not persisted to localStorage like the Models sub-tab is. **HONEST LIMITS: nothing is run-verified against a live runner** — the merge is executed in tests and the wire names come out of the research report's symbol extraction of our own pinned `libllama-server-impl.dylib` and the installed MLX venv sources, but no real generation was performed here; and the *effect* of 0.7-vs-0.8 or 1.1-vs-nothing on output quality is unmeasured. **Files: `bridge/app.py`** (the `SAMPLING_*` block + `sampling_engine`/`_sampling_num`/`_sampling_stop`/`sampling_saved`/`sampling_merge`/`sampling_view` + `POST /api/models/settings`, all immediately above `chat_direct`; the two new keys in `api_models`' installed view; the 3-line direct-lane wiring), **`bridge/panel/index.html`** (`renderSampling`/`toggleSampling`/`samplingSet`/`samplingResetAll`/`samplingPost`/`samplingErr`/`samplingTyping` + one line in `renderDetail` + one in `renderModels`), **`scripts/seed_registry.py`** (USER_KEYS), **`scripts/start_component.sh`** (comment only — no argv change), new **`bridge/tests/test_model_settings.py`**. No vendor edits, no commit. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → **load an MLX model and ask for something long** ("write me 900 words about X") → **the reply no longer stops dead around 512 tokens** (this is the bug fix and it needs no settings at all). Then **Models → pick a model → `▸ Sampling`** → the group lists every field the engine honours with its real name (an MLX model shows **`repetition_penalty`**, a gguf model shows **`repeat_penalty`**) → set **temperature `0.2`** → the header reads `· 1 changed` and a `↺` appears on that row → send a couple of Chat-lane messages and ask the same question twice: **the answers should be visibly more alike than at 0.7** → click **`↺`** (or clear the box) → back to `default 0.7`. Then click **RESCAN** and reopen the group → **the override is still there** (that is the USER_KEYS half). Finally set temperature to `99` → it is refused inline with the range, not silently stored.

> **📦 FAT DMG REBUILT + FIRST-RUN PROVISIONING FINALLY VERIFIED + ONE REAL DESIGN FLAW FOUND (2026-08-20, Opus-5 builder):** `./scripts/build_app.sh --fat` run clean on Debi's Mac — wheelhouse **215 wheels / 301M**, seed **96M**, app payload **417M**, ad-hoc signed, `dist/Harness.dmg` produced. The new bundle carries TODAY'S pins (mlx-vlm 0.6.13, mlx-audio 0.4.8 — note the wheelhouse resolved mlx-audio **0.5.0** as an mlx-vlm dependency, worth watching at the next install; hermes v2026.8.13 with prebuilt web_dist, llama.cpp b10427) plus every Swift change (split v2, tab drag, ghosts, downloads, the auto-reload poll) and the mic `Info.plist` baked in rather than hand-patched. `/Applications/Harness.app` was replaced with it. **§G-phase2/phase3 FIRST-RUN IS NOW VERIFIED — the biggest untested surface since July.** Debi ran the optional clean-machine test (`mv ~/Library/Application\ Support/Harness → Harness.SAVED`, then launch) and the offline provisioner **worked end to end**: five venvs built from the bundled wheelhouse (mlx 706M, odysseus 392M, hermes 285M, searxng 110M, bridge 42M) + bundled CPython + llama.cpp, no terminal, no network, Mission Control all-green. **⚠️ BUT IT EXPOSED A GENUINE DESIGN FLAW, and this is the item for Fable: the provisioner seeds from whatever bundle happens to be in /Applications, with NO check that its seed is at least as new as the install it is replacing.** The app there was an early-August build, so the fresh provision restored a **pre-voice manifest (harness.yaml dated Aug 6, 3 components)** and a pre-voice `vendor/` — voicestudio and voicebox vanished from Mission Control and their tabs served bare API JSON (`{"message":"voicebox API"}` / `{"detail":"Not Found"}`) because the still-running processes had no SPA on disk. Nothing was lost: the provisioner moves the old snapshot aside rather than deleting it, so the restore was a swap (`mv Harness Harness.FRESHTEST && mv Harness.SAVED Harness`) + relaunch → 6/6 running, 6/6 installed, both voice SPAs rendering. **SUGGESTED GUARD (noted, NOT built): the provisioner should refuse to seed over an existing snapshot whose harness.yaml declares MORE components than the seed's — or at minimum compare a build stamp and warn loudly.** Diagnostic sequence that identified it, worth reusing: `.provisioned` mtime (proves a re-provision ran), `harness.yaml` mtime (identifies which seed it came from), presence of `data/<component>-venv`, and `ls ~/Library/Application\ Support/ | grep -i harness` (finds the moved-aside copy). Also confirmed along the way: `build_app.sh --fat` only ever writes into `dist/` (stages `dist/.fatseed`, tars into the bundle) — it never touches a live snapshot; and the bridge enumerates Mission Control cards from **every** `components` entry in harness.yaml with no filtering (`app.py:353`), so a missing card always means a missing manifest entry.

> **🔁 HERMES AUTO-RELOAD NOW COVERS SPLIT VIEW (2026-08-15, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi tested the config-generation auto-reload with **Mission Control and Hermes side by side in split view** — toggled Web Search on, waited 5 minutes, Hermes's Skills page still said `inactive` until ⌘R. **Not a defect in the mechanism, a gap in its TRIGGER:** the reload was only ever called from `maybeReloadStaleHermes(_ idx:)`, which fires when a tab *becomes* visible; a Hermes pane already sitting beside the panel never becomes visible. And side-by-side is exactly how Debi cross-checks the two surfaces, so the uncovered case was the important one. **FIX (app/main.swift only):** a **visibility-gated 4s poll** (⚠️ builder number) that calls the SAME `syncHermesGen(reloadIfNewer:)` — no second copy of the fetch/compare logic (test-pinned: the poll body contains no `URLSession` and no `hermes_config_gen`). New `visibleHermesWebViews()` returns every Hermes surface actually on screen — the primary when a non-ghost pane holds tab 2, **plus** `secondInstances[2]` when a ghost pane does — and `updateHermesGenTimer()` arms the timer iff that set is non-empty, invalidates+nils it otherwise, refuses to restack, and the tick retires itself when the set empties. Armed from the single site `applyPanes()` (every path that changes what is on screen funnels there, and `ensureLoaded` runs before it). The tab-select call site is KEPT so a switch checks immediately instead of waiting a tick. **Both a primary and a ghost are reloaded** when both panes show Hermes ⚠️ (a reload discards that surface's in-page state). Every fail-safe is unchanged and re-asserted: error/non-200/unparseable/missing-field → do nothing; record-before-decide so it cannot loop; `nil` = no claim; a bridge restart back to 0 is recorded silently; 2.0s timeout; off the UI thread; the 600s staleness rule untouched and independent. Log line gained a reason suffix — `[hermes] reload -> config generation N (poll|tab)` — plus `[hermes] gen poll -> on (every 4s)` / `-> off (no Hermes on screen)`. **Panel copy updated to the new truth:** the toolset and skills group descriptions now say the harness reloads that tab for you within a few seconds, split view included, and name the one genuinely uncovered case — a Hermes dashboard open in an outside browser. **VALIDATION: 27 py + 18 js suites green, contract 105, CSS 529/529 zero new rules, ast OK, both panel JS blocks node --check clean, Swift string/comment-aware balance braces 320/320 parens 870/870 brackets 65/65 depth 0.** `test_audio_drop.js` +14 checks (one poll, one arming site, visibility gate, invalidate-and-nil, no restack, self-retiring tick, `[weak self]`, tab-select site kept, `staleAfter` absent from the new code, ghost+primary both in the visible set, all targets reloaded); four existing copy-pins updated honestly because they pinned the old "split view is not covered" promise. ⚠️ **PENDING FABLE QA:** the 4s interval; not gated on window occlusion (a covered/minimised window keeps polling — one loopback GET); the timer also runs while the Hermes page is in `failedLoads` (polls with nothing to do until it recovers); reloading a ghost resets state the user may have navigated separately; the log-line suffix; the panel copy now promising the reload outright. **Swift is compile-unverified as always — `./scripts/ship.sh` is the compile check.**

> **🔁 STANDING OPS RULE EXTENDED — SHIPPING ≠ RESTARTING A COMPONENT (2026-08-15, Debi caught it):** `./scripts/ship.sh` is still THE ship path for OUR code (panel/bridge/scripts/guards/policies + the Swift shell), but it **deliberately leaves components running** ("components stay up"), so after any VENDORED upgrade the old process keeps serving — Debi upgraded Hermes to 0.20.1 and then tested against a **live v0.19.1**, which invalidated the whole verification (no approval card, a write refused, and a typed question delivered mid-turn as an `[OUT-OF-BAND USER MESSAGE]` because the turn was still blocked). **THE RULE: after upgrading a component, restart THAT component with ONE command, run FROM THE SNAPSHOT** (the app's venv is the upgraded one; the repo's is a different install): `cd ~/Library/Application\ Support/Harness && ./scripts/start_component.sh hermes`. That script is a full restart by construction (`hermes dashboard --stop` → `pkill -f "hermes (dashboard|serve)"` → listener-scoped `lsof` port kill → **re-seeds guards/harness-path-guard into ~/.hermes/plugins + plugins.enabled** → re-patches model.* to the runner → relaunch with HERMES_DESKTOP=1), so it does strictly more than the panel's Stop/Start buttons. Same shape for any component name. **VERSION CHECK IS THE PROOF:** the Hermes tab's sidebar footer prints the running version — v0.19.1 vs v0.20.1 told the whole story. ⚠️ **ALSO RE-CHECK `approvals.mode` AFTER A HERMES BUMP:** upstream's default is `smart` (a guardian model auto-decides and shows NO card); Debi set it to `manual` on 2026-07-31 and a config reset would silently reintroduce "it didn't ask for permission". **🧠 FABLE ITEM (noted, NOT built):** give ship.sh a sanctioned `--restart <component>` (or make it warn when a component's running version ≠ its installed version) so this cannot be forgotten again — it is the same class as the contract-gate gap: a manual step that is easy to skip and silently invalidates a verification.

> **🧪 THE CONTRACT GATE IS UNRUNNABLE ON THE MAC — NOTED FOR FABLE, NOT FIXED (2026-08-15, Opus-5 builder; Debi's instruction: note it, do not implement):** during the Hermes v2026.8.13 bump the mandatory gate `python -m pytest bridge/contract_tests/` printed **"No module named pytest"** and Debi continued — i.e. **a vendored pin bump shipped with its gate silently skipped** (it was run in the sandbox afterwards and caught 3 real failures, one behavioural). Root cause: neither the system python NOR `data/bridge-venv` has pytest, and **`data/bridge-venv` has no `pip` module either** because `bootstrap.sh:87` creates it with `uv venv` (no `--seed`) and installs deps via `uv pip install -r bridge/requirements.txt` — so the obvious `python -m pip install pytest` ALSO fails. **NO CODE WAS CHANGED** (`bridge/requirements.txt` untouched; a one-line edit adding `pytest>=8.0` was made and then REVERTED on Debi's instruction). **Options recorded in the roadmap §1c for Fable:** (A) `pytest>=8.0` in `bridge/requirements.txt` — bootstrap installs it and `build_app.sh` 4b pulls it into the fat wheelhouse for free, cost = a test dep in the runtime file; (B) a separate dev-requirements — cleaner, costs a second install path in bootstrap AND the fat installer; (C) orthogonal and arguably the real fix — make ship.sh (or a new scripts/verify.sh) RUN the contract suite and REFUSE LOUDLY when it cannot, so the gate cannot be skipped by accident regardless of where pytest lives. **Interim command for an existing install** (mirrors bootstrap; `python -m pip` cannot work): `source data/bridge-venv/bin/activate && uv pip install pytest && deactivate`.

> **⬆️ UPDATE PASSES RUN BY DEBI + PIN-BUMP RECONCILIATION (2026-08-15, Opus-5 builder; ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi ran all three passes back-to-back. **PASS 1 (MLX pair) FULLY DONE** — snapshot AND repo venvs both report mlx-vlm 0.6.13 / mlx-audio 0.4.8 (mlx-lm 0.31.3, mlx-whisper 0.4.3 unchanged). **PASS 3 (llama.cpp b10427) DONE** — both venvs installed, `--version` prints build 10427 commit 650913862; ⚠️ the SNAPSHOT's rollback copy was NOT made (see the zsh gotcha below), the repo's `data/llamacpp.b10295.bak` exists; rollback remains available by setting the pin back and re-running install_llamacpp.sh (needs network). **PASS 2 (Hermes v2026.7.30 → v2026.8.13) IS INCOMPLETE AND MUST BE FINISHED:** the submodule checkout landed (`v2026.8.13` = f80f453ae0, hermes-agent 0.20.1) and the REPO venv reinstalled cleanly (aiohttp 3.14.1→3.14.3, cryptography 48→50, mcp 1.26→1.28.1, nemo-relay 0.6→0.7.3, pillow 12.2→12.3, web_dist rebuilt), but **the SNAPSHOT venv's `pip install -e` FAILED** (`ERROR: Invalid requirement: '#'`) so the fat app's hermes venv still carries 0.19.1's dependency set while its rsync'd source is 0.20.1 — new code, old deps. Debi must re-run that ONE command. **‼️ NEW STANDING OPS GOTCHA (root cause of BOTH failures): interactive zsh does NOT treat `#` as a comment** (unlike bash; `interactive_comments` is off by default), so any trailing `# note` in a runbook line is passed as ARGUMENTS. It broke `pip install -e … # online` (pass 2) and `cp -R … # THIS IS THE ROLLBACK` (pass 3, hence no snapshot backup), and produced the harmless `grep: #: No such file` noise in pass 1. **RULE: never put a trailing comment on a paste-able command line; put notes on their own line above the block.** **THE CONTRACT GATE WAS SKIPPED** on the Mac (`python3 -m pytest` → "No module named pytest"; the system python has none — **the correct invocation is `data/bridge-venv/bin/python -m pytest bridge/contract_tests/ -q`**), so it was run in the sandbox afterwards: **99/102 passed, 3 failed**, all in the toolsets contract — every other pinned seam (clarify, approval, WS gateway, skills API, `/api/tools/toolsets` + `enabled`, path-guard hook) survived the bump untouched. **RECONCILIATION (now green, contract 102 → 105):** (1) **BEHAVIOURAL — upstream added `a2a` to `_DEFAULT_OFF_TOOLSETS`** (`tools_config.py:156`, now eight names); our stale mirror would have made "Hermes's defaults" switch `a2a` ON — the SAME defect class as the Video-Analysis bug, one release later. Mirror corrected + pinned in both directions. Honest scope: latent not live (`a2a` is a bundled platform plugin registered as a deferred loader, `plugins.py:3855`, so the row is normally absent from our catalog). (2) **COSMETIC — the platform parameterisation is a trap for a skim-reader:** `_load_enabled_toolsets(platform)` (`server.py:4267`) takes the SESSION SOURCE, not the `platform_toolsets` key; the config read is still hardcoded `_get_platform_tools(cfg,"cli",…)` (`server.py:4399`). Our bridge sends `session.create {"source":"harness"}` → `_resolve_session_source` returns it verbatim (`server.py:3693`) → platform = **"harness"**, config key unchanged **`platform_toolsets.cli`**. Our lever points at the right key and the UI is not lying; re-pinned to the new chain PLUS a negative that `_get_platform_tools` is never called with the session platform. (3) **COSMETIC — the `project` fold became `sorted(enabled | _gui_surface_toolsets(session_platform))`** (`server.py:4410`, focus path :4293); `_gui_surface_toolsets` (:4246-4264) = `{"project"}` **plus `desktop_ui` iff platform == "desktop"**, so for our "harness" lane the fold is still exactly `project` and the Check card's count stays correct. ⚠️ **SHARP EDGE now pinned both ways: `start_component.sh:645` launches with `HERMES_DESKTOP=1`** (for the cron ticker) and that is exactly what `_resolve_session_platform` turns into `"desktop"` **when no source is given** — our explicit session source is the ONLY thing keeping this lane off the desktop surface, whose `desktop_ui` 8 tools (read_terminal/open_preview/focus_pane/…) need a GUI renderer our panel does not implement; the magic string is promoted to `HERMES_SESSION_SOURCE` (4 sites) and pinned against regressing to a literal. **GAP CLOSED BEYOND THE BRIEF: `session.active_list`'s status vocabulary was never contract-pinned** (our own docstring flagged it) — it drives the relay liveness probe, so an unrecognised busy status would cut a WORKING session off mid-answer with `· interrupted`; verified unchanged (`waiting|starting|working|idle`, `server.py:8040`) and now pinned as an exact set, with `session.active_list`/`session.interrupt` added to WS_METHODS. Also re-verified intact: `_toolset_configuration_platform`, `_CONFIG_ONLY_TOOLSETS` (mirror byte-identical), `_save_platform_tools`, `has_explicit_config`/empty-list-means-everything (so our empty-set refusal still stands), and all 11 JSON-RPC methods we call. **VALIDATION: 27 py + 18 js suites green, contract 105, toolsets unit 239 checks, ast OK, harness.yaml parses + the awk pin reader returns v2026.8.13, both panel JS blocks node --check clean, CSS 529/529.** ⚠️ **PENDING FABLE QA:** `HERMES_SESSION_SOURCE` as a new contract-load-bearing constant; the defaults-preset turning a user-enabled `a2a` back off (reset semantics, identical to video/spotify, the opposite reading is arguable); `hermes_tool_summary` counting `project` conditional on our source rather than an unconditional upstream literal; the WS contract method-list extension (Fable-lane surface). **Files: `bridge/app.py`, `bridge/contract_tests/test_hermes_toolsets_contract.py`, `test_hermes_ws_contract.py`, `bridge/tests/test_hermes_toolsets.py`, `harness.yaml` (reconciliation recorded where the next bumper reads it).** No panel edit, no vendor edit.

> **🔧 OPUS-5 TAKEOVER: TOOLSET BUG ROOT-CAUSED (3 DEFECTS, NOT THE PLATFORM HYPOTHESIS) + B2 PER-SKILL LEVER + HERMES AUTO-RELOAD + B5 RESEARCH (2026-08-15, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's gates: fix the live bug first, B3 measurement-only, B4 skipped. **THE LIVE BUG — FABLE'S PLATFORM HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED, and the real causes are worse.** `GET /api/tools/toolsets` does NOT serve one fixed platform: it resolves per row via `_toolset_configuration_platform` (`tools_config.py:202-228`, default `cli`, only discord/discord_admin diverge) and the PUT uses the IDENTICAL call (`web_routers/tools.py:69-76,96,100,123,166`); Hermes's own Skills page reads the same endpoint and the same `enabled` field (`web/src/lib/api.ts:777`, `SkillsPage.tsx:163,596-601`), and the Configure modal's "Disabled for CLI" is `toolset.platform_label` printed beside the SAME toggle (`ToolsetConfigDrawer.tsx:214,254-262`) — i.e. it CONFIRMS those rows are the cli lane. The two surfaces cannot structurally disagree. **DEFECT 1 (explains Video Analysis ON that Debi never enabled): our "Everything back on" wrote EVERY catalog name, but upstream keeps seven deliberately off** — `_DEFAULT_OFF_TOOLSETS = {homeassistant, spotify, discord, discord_admin, video, video_gen, x_search}` (`tools_config.py:155`), subtracted when it expands `hermes-cli` (:2319-2347). One press of a button promising to "restore Hermes's surface" went PAST it. Same path also issued `PUT stt {enabled:false}` — `stt` is `_CONFIG_ONLY_TOOLSETS` (:164) with ZERO tool schemas, so "Minimal preset" was disabling Hermes's speech-to-text for no prompt saving at all. **DEFECT 2 (explains "toggling changes nothing there"): Hermes's own page never refreshes** — `SkillsPage.tsx:155-174` fetches once on mount (`useEffect` keyed only on profile), `:180-186` patches its own state optimistically after ITS toggles; our shell only reloaded that webview after 600s backgrounded (`maybeReloadStaleHermes`/`staleAfter`). Debi was reading a frozen page in both directions. **DEFECT 3: the empty-set guard was defeatable** — it tested `desired` against ALL known names, so an `stt` or discord row satisfied it while every real cli toolset went off, landing `platform_toolsets.cli: []` = *enable everything* (:2231 → `server.py:3915 return None`). **FIXES:** new scope layer in `bridge/app.py` (`HERMES_LEVER_PLATFORM`, mirrored `HERMES_CONFIG_ONLY_TOOLSETS`/`HERMES_DEFAULT_OFF_TOOLSETS` both contract-pinned byte-identical upstream, `hermes_toolset_platform` fail-open, `hermes_lever_*`, scope-aware `hermes_toolset_plan/valid`); `"all"` preset now means **Hermes's own defaults** (chip renamed "Hermes's defaults") ⚠️ redefinition; non-lever rows still render but carry `not a prompt toolset` / `Discord only` pills and never move under a preset; counts/Check card are lane-scoped and NAME the cli platform. **AUTO-RELOAD (defect 2's honest fix, replacing "press ⌘R"):** new process-lifetime **Hermes config generation** counter in the bridge, bumped on every SUCCESSFUL write to Hermes's config (toolset POST, skills POST, `_hermes_write_mcp` after `os.replace`), published on the EXISTING `/api/status` (no new route — `portOpen` already fetches that URL); `app/main.swift` extends `maybeReloadStaleHermes` (600s rule untouched and independent) to reload the Hermes webview when the generation INCREASED since that webview loaded — async 2s URLSession, fail-safe on every error/missing field, records-before-deciding so it cannot loop, `[hermes] reload -> config generation N` slog. ⚠️ split view (Hermes open BESIDE the panel) is genuinely uncovered and the panel copy says so rather than overclaiming. **B2 PER-SKILL TRIMMING SHIPPED:** upstream API exists → we write through it (`PUT /api/skills/toggle {name,enabled,profile?}`, `web_routers/skills.py:426-437`, model `web_models.py:604-607`; read `GET /api/skills` :394-423). **TWO MEMORY CORRECTIONS:** (a) the recorded gotcha "frontmatter names, NOT directory names" is TOO STRONG at this pin — the prompt builder skips on EITHER (`agent/prompt_builder.py:1654,1676,1759`) and the listing's name is frontmatter-with-directory-fallback (`tools/skills_tool.py:737`); the safe rule is unchanged: echo the listing's own string back verbatim, never derive one. (b) **per-skill scope is GLOBAL, not platform-scoped** — the handler calls `save_disabled_skills` with no platform (`skills_config.py:64-72`) and `SkillToggle` has no platform field, so a skill switched off here is off on EVERY Hermes surface (said plainly in the UI); `skills.platform_disabled.<platform>` exists (`skill_utils.py:420-455`) but Hermes's own dashboard never writes it, so neither do we — we READ it and pill the rows it hides. Upstream ships no default-disabled skills at this pin (`config_defaults.py:1682`), pinned so the day it does, the chip doesn't repeat defect 1. UI: collapsible `▸ per-skill (78 installed)` under the skills row, lazy-fetched on expand (⚠️ not with every Capabilities render — 78 rows on every poll was the wrong default), filter box, `N of M skills in the prompt`, per-row switches, "Turn off the shown" (bulk, scoped to the FILTERED names explicitly, tested that hidden rows are untouched ⚠️), "Hermes's defaults"; zero new CSS. ⚠️ skills-toolset-OFF is a NOTE not a lock (writes still shape the index for when it returns); no empty-set refusal (unlike toolsets, `skills.disabled` has no inversion); N sequential PUTs (no bulk route at this pin, contract-pinned). Also fixed a LATENT test defect: `test_hermes_toolsets.js`'s `grab()` extractor silently returned a TRUNCATED function on any body containing an apostrophe-in-comment or a nested template literal — it only worked by luck; now comment/template aware. **B5 BARGE-IN RESEARCH (report only, `docs/research/2026-08-15-bargein-feasibility.md`):** the spec floor for `echoCancellation:true` is **`remote-only`** — a conforming browser need only cancel WebRTC peer audio, and our TTS is a blob through `new Audio()`, so `true` GUARANTEES US NOTHING; Chromium is documented remote-only, while WebKit/macOS routes through CoreAudio `VoiceProcessingIO` (device-level, structurally cannot tell peer audio from an `<audio>` element) which ARGUES Safari is effectively `"all"` — unresolvable from documents. Two code facts fell out: **the panel sets NO echoCancellation constraint anywhere** (all four getUserMedia sites are bare `{audio:true}` — an unmeasured default), and `vadStep` teaches its floor from every non-speech block, so any un-gating during playback needs a SEPARATE frozen-floor segmenter or the VAD goes deaf next window. RECOMMENDATION: green-light (1) a ⌘K AEC probe in the shape of the shipped worklet spike (with an `echoCancellation:false` control leg — without it a low number is ambiguous), and (2) **keyboard push-to-interrupt (~20 lines, no mic, cannot regress the feedback invariant)** — `stopSpeaking` already funnels to `onDone`→grace→listening, it only lacks a key binding. Acoustic barge-in stays CONDITIONAL on the probe showing residual below `VAD_FLOOR` (0.006); WASM AEC refused (frame alignment, not licence/size). **B3 (persistent STT worker) NOT BUILT — gated per spec**; Debi has the §3.4 measurement block. **B4 (silero) SKIPPED per Debi this round.** **VALIDATION: 27 py + 18 js suites green, contract 92 → 102, ast OK, CSS 521/521 zero new rules, both panel JS blocks node --check clean, Swift string-aware balance braces 306/306 parens 749/749 brackets 57/57 depth 0.** **HONEST LIMITS: nothing is run-verified against a live Hermes** (no route from the sandbox to :9119, `~/.hermes/config.yaml` outside the mount) — every claim is read out of the vendored source at the pin and contract-pinned; **the Swift half is compile-unverified** (no swiftc; `./scripts/ship.sh` IS the compile check). **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** the `"all"`-preset redefinition + chip rename; two upstream constants mirrored in app.py; non-lever rows staying flippable by deliberate click; `hermes_toolset_platform` failing open to cli; `/api/status` chosen as the generation carrier (not the cheapest route, but the one the shell already calls) and it port-probes every component; a reload discards in-page Hermes state; the mcp bump being broader than the reported bug; bump-per-write not per-action; skills bulk action + lazy fetch + note-not-lock; the 2.0s timeout and nil-means-no-claim.

> **‼️ HERMES TOOLSET CORRECTNESS — RECONCILE ON RENDER + A ONE-CLICK `Check` THAT ANSWERS "WHAT DOES HERMES ACTUALLY HAND THE MODEL?" (2026-08-15, Opus-5 builder to FABLE-SPECS-2026-08-15 B1; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's top item, and the complaint was about TRUST, not a bug: she could not tell whether our Capabilities switches match what Hermes gives the model, because Hermes's own Skills→**ALL** page says "78/78 enabled" while our lever says minimal — so she had to open a second app and find the Skills→**TOOLSETS** page to confirm we were right. **FIRST FINDING, and it reframes the slice: our switches were ALREADY reconciled and always have been.** `renderHermesTools` renders `snap.toolsets[].enabled`, which is upstream's own computed field (`hermes_cli/web_routers/tools.py:97` `"enabled": is_enabled`, from `_get_platform_tools` via `enabled_by_platform[target_platform]`, tools_config.py:2195) re-read on every load and after every write — **we never render our last write.** So there was nothing to fix in the switch; what was missing is (a) a visible signal when Hermes's answer is NOT the one we asked for, and (b) a way to see the resulting NUMBER without leaving the app. **(1) DRIFT, ONE DIRECTION ONLY, AND THAT RESTRAINT IS THE DESIGN.** New PURE `hermes_toolset_drift(rows, cfg)`: INTENT is the PERSISTED one — every write goes through Hermes's own `PUT` → `_save_platform_tools` (tools_config.py:2491), so `platform_toolsets.cli` on disk IS the record of what this panel asked for — and TRUTH is the probe's `enabled`. A name **listed but reported off** is unambiguous drift (its causes are real: `agent.disabled_toolsets` is subtracted LAST, tools_config.py:2455; `agent.coding_context:"focus"` short-circuits the list entirely, tui_gateway/server.py:3797). **The REVERSE direction is deliberately NOT flagged**, because two legitimate causes are indistinguishable from the payload: a composite name like `hermes-cli` in the same list EXPANDS to more toolsets (tools_config.py:2240-2260), and the config-only toolsets (`_CONFIG_ONLY_TOOLSETS = {"stt"}`, tools_config.py:164) are enabled by their own config section and never appear in the list at all — flagging those would fire a scary pill on a healthy install, and detecting them by name is exactly the heuristic the spec forbids. That direction is instead covered panel-side by `hermesToolsIntent`, our own in-session record of what we asked for, which cannot be wrong about itself; a **failed** write deletes its entry (a write that never landed is not an ask). Rows whose configuration platform is not `cli` are skipped (discord persists to `platform_toolsets.discord`, `_toolset_configuration_platform`, tools_config.py:216), and no saved list at all ⇒ **no claim**. The row shows `out of sync — Hermes says on/off` in the existing `.cap-pill off` grammar, the group header shows `N out of sync`, and ONE **`Adopt Hermes's state`** chip appears — it clears our claim, **re-reads and re-renders, and writes nothing**, because the honest response to "Hermes disagrees" is to believe Hermes. ⚠️ if the disagreement is the PERSISTED one the pill correctly survives adopt: that half is not ours to clear and hiding it would be the lie being removed. **(2) THE `Check` CHIP — the number, live, in one click.** New `GET /api/hermes/toolsets/summary` (fresh probe, never the panel snapshot, 409 when Hermes is down) + PURE `hermes_tool_summary(rows, skills)` → an inline card reading **"Hermes will hand the model N tools: <names>"** plus **"skill index: PRESENT/ABSENT"**. Three things make the number honest rather than plausible, all read out of the pin: **(a)** the union is DE-DUPLICATED across enabled toolsets (`resolve_toolset`, toolsets.py:719, composes the same way); **(b)** the lane's resolver folds in the **`project`** toolset unconditionally — `return sorted(enabled | {"project"})`, **tui_gateway/server.py:3921** — and `project` is NOT in `CONFIGURABLE_TOOLSETS` (tools_config.py:95-122) so it has no row and no switch, meaning a naive count is short by its **3 tools** (`project_list/create/switch`, toolsets.py:254-257); they are counted and reported SEPARATELY with the reason; **(c)** the listing resolves with `include_default_mcp_servers=False` (tools.py:76) while the runtime resolve uses `True` (server.py:3910), so **MCP tools are not counted and the card says so**. The skill-index verdict is upstream's OWN predicate computed over **TOOL names** — `has_skills_tools = any(name in agent.valid_tool_names for name in ['skills_list','skill_view','skill_manage'])` (**agent/system_prompt.py:299**, else `skills_prompt = ""` at :326) — deliberately not the toolset NAME, so a rename upstream cannot make us claim an index that is not there. The comparable numbers Debi sees in Hermes are `summary_parts = [f"{len(tools)} tools", f"{total_skills} skills"]` (**hermes_cli/banner.py:822**), whose skills half is itself gated on `"skills" in _enabled_ts` (**banner.py:780**, printing `Skills toolset disabled` and **0 skills** while the library on disk is untouched). ⚠️ **FOCUS MODE MADE THE CARD DANGEROUS AND IS HANDLED:** with `agent.coding_context:"focus"` the resolver returns its own selection before the config list is read, so the figures do not apply — the endpoint returns `focus_override` and the card prints a red line saying the numbers are NOT what Hermes will hand the model, rather than printing a confident wrong count (the exact failure this affordance exists to abolish). **(3) THE TWO LAYERS, SAID ON THE ROW:** the skills row now carries one sentence — *"Removes the skill index from the model's prompt — the skill library in Hermes stays installed and enabled (its Skills page will still say N/N)"* — which is precisely the sentence whose absence sent Debi to a second app. The group description also states outright that every switch shows what HERMES reports, re-read on every refresh, not what we last asked for. **ZERO new CSS** (`.cap-pill off` / `.cap-btn` / `.cap-row` / `.cap-desc` / `.cap-err`; CSS balance 528/528 unchanged, no rule added). **TESTS: `test_hermes_toolsets.py` 131 → 175 checks** (the full drift table incl. every fail-open case, the deliberately-unflagged direction asserted as a NEGATIVE, the other-platform skip, junk totality; the summary's dedupe, the project fold not double-counting a tool already present, the tool-name-not-toolset-name skill gate, the 409, the focus flag, and wiring greps that the summary re-probes and writes nothing) **+ `test_hermes_toolsets.js` 40 → 81** (the sync table incl. "no intent and no drift ⇒ no claim" and a stale drift flag not firing against a row now reported ON, both card states, the focus warning, exactly ONE adopt chip, adopt asserted to contain no POST, and a negative that the preset path records no client intent) **+ contract 79 → 85** (`enabled` and `tools` field names and their producers, `available` pinned as a literal alias of `enabled`, the `project` fold AND its three tools AND that `project` is still not configurable, the three-name skills gate, the banner's summary line, and the MCP-inclusion asymmetry). **VALIDATION: all 25 py + 17 js suites green, contract 85, ast OK, harness.yaml parses, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** ⚠️ **PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) drift is deliberately one-directional (reasoned above) — the cost is that a toolset switched on outside our panel is not flagged, only one switched-off-against-our-ask; (b) `Adopt Hermes's state` cannot clear a persisted disagreement, so on a `agent.disabled_toolsets` install the chip stays put (its tooltip says it re-reads and writes nothing); (c) the summary's honest total counts `project` but **not** MCP tools, so it will read LOWER than Hermes's own banner on any install with connected MCP servers — the card states this, but the two numbers will not always be equal; (d) the summary is computed from the toolsets listing, **not** from the gateway's `session.info` (server.py:4820-4830), which is the true `agent.tools` — that would need a built session and the prefill it costs, and returns `{}` until the deferred build lands; (e) the verify card is dropped on any successful write rather than recomputed (Check re-reads); (f) `focus_override` is now read on two endpoints. **HONEST LIMITS: nothing is run-verified against a live Hermes** — the drift/summary shapes come from a fake dashboard in the unit tests plus contract pins on the real vendored source, and the equality of our N with the banner's N is an argued claim, not a measured one. **Files: `bridge/app.py`** (`hermes_toolset_drift`, `hermes_tool_summary`, `HERMES_GATEWAY_ALWAYS_TOOLSET/_TOOLS`, `HERMES_SKILL_INDEX_TOOLS`, `hermes_toolset_view(rows, skills, cfg)`, `GET /api/hermes/toolsets/summary`), **`bridge/panel/index.html`** (`hermesToolsIntent`, `hermesToolsetSync`, `hermesToolsetExtra`, `renderHermesToolsCheck`, `hermesToolsAdopt`, `hermesToolsCheck`, `refreshHermesToolsRender`), the three test files. No vendor edits, no commit. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → **Capabilities → Tools → Hermes tools** → click **Minimal preset**, then **Check** → the card says **"Hermes will hand the model ~12 tools"**, names them, and reads **`skill index: ABSENT — the 78 skills stay installed in Hermes, but none of them are in the model's prompt`** → now open **Chat → HERMES → ＋ New** and compare with Hermes's own banner on that new chat: the tools figure should agree once you add any MCP tools (the card says it cannot see those). Then click **Everything back on** → **Check** again → the number jumps and the skill index reads **PRESENT**. Finally, to see the reconcile: in **Hermes's own** Skills → TOOLSETS page switch one toolset OFF, come back to our panel and refresh the section → that row shows **`out of sync — Hermes says off`** with **`Adopt Hermes's state`** at the top → click it → the pill clears and the switch shows Hermes's answer. (Nothing is written at any point by Adopt.)

> **▣ COMPACT CHROME — THIRD FAILURE ROOT-CAUSED (IT WAS THE VALUES, NOT THE CASCADE) + SEGMENT-CLOCK MAX-TURN GUARD + TOOLSET SETUP PILL (2026-08-14h, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** three slices, no vendor edit, no commit. **TASK 1 — THE CSS WAS FINE ALL ALONG, AND THIS TIME THAT IS PROVEN RATHER THAN ARGUED.** Two previous sessions diagnosed "▣ turns gold and nothing else changes" from the stylesheet (first: coverage; second: coverage again, wider) and both were wrong. This session stopped reasoning from selectors and established the structural facts by walking the real bytes: the compact block sits at **absolute offset 62558-69166, inside the FIRST `<style>` element (155-69434)**; a comment-and-string-aware tokenizer puts **CSS brace depth at exactly 0 where the block starts**, parses **all 24 of its rules at depth 0**, finds the block **last in the sheet**, finds **111/111 balanced comments** and **zero `!important`** on any chip/button, **no `@media`/`@supports` wrapper**, **no inline `style=` writes** to any of these controls (only `h.style.font` at :7716, unrelated), and **no WKUserScript injected into the panel** (`app/main.swift:408` injects into the ODYSSEUS webview only). And the gold ▣ is itself the proof the attribute lands: `#chrome-chip`'s gold has **exactly one source in the whole sheet — a compact rule (`:915`)** — so if the chip goes gold, `data-chrome="compact"` IS set and the block IS live. **THE REAL DEFECT IS THAT THE RULES WON AND MEANT NOTHING — the axis promised "solid ground, firm border" and then chose values at or below the visibility floor.** Measured, not asserted: the ground was `--card2` **#181527** sitting on a `--card` **#14121d** card = **contrast ratio 1.04** (a 4/3/10 RGB lift — invisible on a dark panel), and against `--bg2` **1.09**; the "firm border" was `border-color:var(--line2)`, which is **byte-identical to what the base `button` rule already sets — a literal no-op**; and hover resolved to `--card`, which is **DARKER than the resting `--card2`**, i.e. the wrong direction. Everything else in the axis moved ≤1px. So the fix is not more coverage and not more specificity: the axis now owns **three namespaced surface tokens** — `--chrome-btn/-hi/-edge` = **#332b50 / #453b6b / #574b83** dark, **#e3d9c0 / #d5c7a6 / #b9a878** light (one new rule; the light hover DARKENS, which is correct on paper and is asserted as the deliberate inversion) — chosen against measured ratios: ground **1.41 vs --card / 1.48 vs --bg2**, `--fg` on it **7.7:1**, edge **1.84 vs --line2**, hover strictly lighter. `color:var(--fg)` replaces `--dim` on the chip families because `--dim` on the new ground is only **2.9:1**, which would have been a readability regression. **THREE SPECIFICITY LANDMINES CAUGHT WHILE MAKING THE VALUES REAL, ALL THE SAME CLASS AND ALL SELF-INFLICTED BY THE FIX:** (a) `html[…] button` is **(0,1,2)** and OUTRANKS base `button.primary` **(0,1,1)**, so a `color` on the bare-button rule would have turned the one cream-filled button **light-on-light** — the declaration is dropped and a test now pins `button.primary` resolving to `#171420`; (b) compact `.cap-btn` **(0,2,1)** outranks `.cap-btn.arm`/`.go` **(0,2,0)**, so the two-step-delete **✕ would have stopped turning red** — both states are RESTATED in the block; (c) compact `#chat-model-btn` **(1,1,1)** outranks `#chat-model-btn.empty` **(1,1,0)**, so "nothing selected" would have stopped reading faint — restated too. **PLUS THE THING THAT MAKES THE NEXT REPORT GROUND TRUTH: a permanent RUNTIME SELF-CHECK.** New pure-observer `chromeProbe()` reads the attribute **BACK** off `<html>` (`getAttribute`, not the variable just written) and asks **`getComputedStyle`** on a real control (`#mode-chat` → `#cards button` → `.chip`) for radius / background / border / font / text-transform, and `toggleChrome()` emits it to **both the console and the activity feed** after the write. One read per click, cannot throw, writes nothing. A stylesheet cannot say what the browser computed; this can. **TESTS: `test_compact_chrome.js` 75 → 134 checks**, and the new ones are the ones that would have caught this: the block's byte range asserted **inside the first `<style>`** and present **exactly once** (not duplicated into an artifact `srcdoc` builder), **brace depth 0 at block start**, no open at-rule, plus a **VALUE-level no-op detector** — the tokens are resolved to hex, luminance/contrast computed in-test, and the axis must clear **1.3:1 against every surface a control sits on**, must not alias `--card`/`--card2`/`--line2`, must hover lighter (dark) / darker (light), and **no compact `background`/`border-color` may restate what the base sheet already computes** (resolved per element through the existing cascade resolver). **Proven to bite: reverting to the shipped values in a scratch copy fails 11 assertions**, incl. `ratio 1.04 >= 1.3` and the border-colour no-op. ⚠️ **ONE HAZARD FOUND IN MY OWN FIRST DRAFT, worth recording: a code COMMENT containing the literal text `<style>` silently truncated the test's own stylesheet parse** (`split('<style>')[1]`) and made every token lookup return undefined — the comment was reworded. **TASK 2 — MAX-TURN → MAX GENERATION SEGMENT (Fable redesign after Debi's correct objection that a total-turn cap punishes legitimate long research).** The guard no longer measures the turn; it measures **ONE UNBROKEN GENERATION STRETCH**. New pure `hermes_segment_resets(ev_type)` (+ `HERMES_SEGMENT_RESET_EVENTS = ("tool.start","tool.complete")`), and the relay **RESETS the clock** on either of those events **and on every approval/clarify resolution** (`_resolved`), on top of the existing **pause while a card is pending**. A reset re-arms `seg_started` and **zeroes the accrued pause**, and it runs **AFTER** the pause bookkeeping and **BEFORE** the event-path overrun check, so a resetting event is never judged against the pre-reset clock. Net effect: **a research turn making a tool call every few minutes runs forever**; only a model deliberating for the whole budget with nothing to show for it is interrupted. **Deltas do NOT reset** — a spiralling model emits continuously, so resetting on deltas would blind the guard to the only thing it exists to catch (test-pinned as a negative over 12 event names). Renamed honestly: `hermes_turn_spent`→`hermes_segment_spent`, `hermes_turn_overrun`→`hermes_segment_overrun`, `_hermes_kill_turn`→`_hermes_kill_segment`, `turn_started`→`seg_started`, log line → `[hermes] generation segment exceeded max_turn_s (Ns >= Ns)`. New message: **"generation exceeded 600s without a tool call or output — likely a deliberation loop; interrupted server-side (hermes.max_turn_s)"**. **UNCHANGED AND NEGATIVE-TESTED:** the config key stays top-level **`hermes.max_turn_s`** (default 600, `0`=off, junk→default — the ship.sh additive-merge reason is unchanged), read **ONCE at submit**, `session.interrupt` as the remedy, the tick check ordered **before** both silence guards, the event check **after** the done-break, and the 60s / 600s / 20s-heartbeat / `active_list` / panel-Stop paths untouched. **`test_hermes_max_turn.py` 83 → 112 checks** (full reset table incl. whitespace/None/int/list totality, the "reset at t=590 cannot overrun at t=600" guarantee, and the wiring/ordering greps). ⚠️ **one pre-existing test defect fixed honestly:** the relay slice was anchored on the FIRST `async def gen():` in app.py, which swallowed the module-level pure functions and made every source-ORDER assertion meaningless; it now anchors on the LAST one before the stop endpoint. **TASK 3 — `needs setup in Hermes` PILL, and the API DOES carry a signal (file:line).** `GET /api/tools/toolsets` rows carry **`configured` (bool)** — `hermes_cli/web_routers/tools.py:107`, produced by `hermes_cli/tools_config.py:2589 _toolset_has_keys` — and it is **exactly the field Hermes's own Skills→TOOLSETS page prints its amber "Setup needed" caption from** (`web/src/pages/SkillsPage.tsx:606`, `ts.enabled && !ts.configured`). So the lever rows now show a faint **`needs setup in Hermes`** pill in the existing `.cap-pill off` grammar — **ZERO new CSS**. The bridge **fails OPEN**: only an explicit `False` warns, so a build or probe shape without the key can never invent a scary pill. ⚠️ **DELIBERATE DEVIATION: not gated on `enabled &&` like upstream** — knowing a toolset needs setup BEFORE you switch it on is the entire point of the row. ⚠️ **HONEST LIMIT, contract-pinned so nobody trusts it more than it deserves: `configured` is OPTIMISTIC.** `_toolset_has_keys` returns True as soon as any provider in the category needs no env vars (Local Browser, Edge TTS), so `browser`/`tts` can report `configured:true` while a one-time `post_setup` install is still missing — the same false negative Hermes's own UI shows. The ACCURATE predicate **`_toolset_needs_configuration_prompt` (tools_config.py:3328)** exists and covers exactly the post_setup / image_gen / video_gen cases, but is **referenced by no web router at all** — a contract test asserts it stays unexposed and tells the next reader to switch the pill to it if that changes. Also recorded: the **CONFIGURE button itself is NOT data-driven** (rendered unconditionally, `SkillsPage.tsx:634-643`), `available` on the listing is a **literal alias of `enabled`** (tools.py:105-106) and not a readiness flag, and the only requirements-bearing endpoint is a **per-toolset fan-out** `GET /api/tools/toolsets/{name}/config` (`providers[].status` / `env_vars[].is_set` / `post_setup`) — deliberately NOT built (N requests per render). **No name heuristics anywhere.** **VALIDATION: all 26 py + 17 js suites green, contract 76 → 79 (three new upstream pins), CSS balance 520/520 (517 + the 3 restatement rules; +4 rules total, all inside the sanctioned block), ast OK, harness.yaml parses, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean, `bash -n` clean on ship.sh.** **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) the six hex surface tokens are **builder values** picked against measured ratios, and they are the first colours this project has added outside `:root`/the light palette — namespaced `--chrome-*` and scoped to the axis, but they ARE new colour; (b) the axis now restates state colours in **three** places (`button.primary`, `.cap-btn.arm/.go`, `#chat-*-btn.empty`) — each is forced by specificity, but it is three second homes for a value; (c) `1.3:1` as the "perceptible" floor and `--fg`-not-`--dim` for chip labels are judgement calls; (d) the self-check writes **two** feed lines per ▣ click (the flip, then the probe) — deliberate, so a screenshot of the feed is a diagnosis, but it is chattier; (e) the segment guard's reset set is **only** tool lifecycle events, so a future non-tool progress signal would need adding there or a legitimately long non-tool phase could still be cut; (f) the error text says "or output" while the reset rule is tool-events-plus-card-resolution — Fable's wording kept verbatim, the nuance is in a code comment; (g) the setup pill shows regardless of switch state (above). **HONEST LIMITS: nothing is run-verified in a browser** — no Chrome bridge was connectable this session (`list_connected_browsers` returned empty), so the contrast claims come from computed luminance over the real tokens rather than `getComputedStyle`, and the segment guard still cannot be exercised without a running gateway and a model that actually spirals. **Files: `bridge/panel/index.html`** (compact block + `chromeProbe`/`toggleChrome` + the toolset pill), **`bridge/app.py`** (segment clock block ≈L4155-4235 + relay ≈L4340-4490; `hermes_toolset_view` `needs_setup`), **`harness.yaml`** (the `hermes:` comment now describes segment semantics), **`bridge/tests/test_compact_chrome.js`**, **`test_hermes_max_turn.py`**, **`test_hermes_toolsets.py`**, **`test_hermes_toolsets.js`**, **`bridge/contract_tests/test_hermes_toolsets_contract.py`**. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → on **Mission Control** click **▣** → **every card's Start / Stop / Logs button gains a clearly lighter slate ground with a visible edge** (this is the part that did nothing three times) → check the **activity feed**: beside `compact chrome on` there is now a second line reading `compact=compact probe=mode-chat radius=5px bg=rgb(51,43,80) …` — **if that line says `compact=null`, or the radius/bg match the editorial values, paste it verbatim: that is the ground truth we have been missing.** Then hover a button (it should get LIGHTER), click **◐** for light theme with ▣ still on (warm ground, hover DARKENS), Chat → the lane chips read **Agent / Chat / Hermes** in sans mixed case on a real ground, ⚙ Capabilities → Tools → the **Add server / Minimal preset** buttons change too and any **✕ delete still turns red when armed**. Then **Chat → HERMES** → send something that makes it spiral → it still dies at the budget; but a long turn that keeps calling tools must now run **past** 10 minutes untouched (set `max_turn_s: 30` in the snapshot's `harness.yaml` to see both quickly). Finally **Capabilities → Tools** → any toolset Hermes reports unconfigured (typically **browser automation / image or video generation**) now carries a faint **`needs setup in Hermes`** pill.
