> **▣ COMPACT CHROME "DOES NOTHING" — ROOT-CAUSED (IT WAS **NOT** SPECIFICITY) + MULTI-SELECT ASK CARDS v2 (2026-08-14f, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** two panel-only slices, zero bridge/vendor change. **PART 1 — THE ▣ TOGGLE WAS A NO-OP WHERE DEBI WAS STANDING, AND THE CAUSE IS COVERAGE, NOT CASCADE.** The reported symptom (the chip renders, turns gold, and nothing else moves) is exactly right, and it is NOT the id-specificity trap the brief suspected: the attribute IS applied (`toggleChrome` at `index.html:1820-1826` sets `documentElement.dataset.chrome`, the same key the pre-paint script reads at `:960`), the block IS live and IS last in the single served `<style>` (there is exactly one panel stylesheet — the other `<style>` strings in the file are artifact-iframe `srcdoc` builders at `:8426+`), and **the block wins every property it declares.** That was not guessed: a cascade resolver was written over the REAL stylesheet and the REAL element shapes, and it confirms `html[data-chrome="compact"] .chip` (0-2-1) beating `.chip` (0-1-0), `.mode-chip`, `.mp-act`, `.caps-tab.on`, and the id-prefixed rows (`#chat-send`, `#chat-model-btn`, `#chat-talk/auto/conv`, `#mode-browse`) all landing — the first cut had ALREADY id-prefixed every control the base sheet styles by id, which is why the specificity hypothesis fails. **THE ACTUAL DEFECT: all 18 rules targeted CHIP CLASSES, and Mission Control — the view the app opens on, and the one Debi was looking at — contains no chips at all beyond the four topbar icons.** Every control on it (Start / Stop / Install / Logs / Retry on each component card, `.md-actions` rows, the download rows, the setup checklist) is an **UNCLASSED `<button>`**, whose base rule (`index.html:151-156`) is ALREADY `-apple-system` 12.5px with `border-radius:9px`, `padding:7px 15px` and `border:1px solid var(--line2)` — so the compact axis's only reach onto that screen was `button.primary { border-radius:8px; border-width:1px }`, i.e. **a 1px radius change plus a border-width that was already 1px: a literal no-op.** The four topbar icon chips do change, by ~2px (26×26 vs a 13px glyph in 6/9 padding) with `--bg2`→`--card2` (#0e0c15→#181527) and radius 8→6 — imperceptible on a dark UI, which is why the gold ▣ (its own `#chrome-chip` rule, the one unmistakable delta) was the only thing she could see. **FIX: 5 rules, all prefixed** — the bare `button` (solid `--card2` ground, `--line2` border, radius 6, 12px/6-13 padding) + `button:hover:not(.primary)`, `.cap-btn` + hover, and `.cmsg .approval .ap-btn`. **TWO TRAPS HANDLED, BOTH PROVEN BY THE RESOLVER RATHER THAN ASSERTED:** (a) the new generic `button` rule TIES with base `button.primary` (both 0-1-1) and would have won on source order, flattening the panel's one filled button — so the cream fill is **restated** on the compact `button.primary` rule (0-2-1), and a test now pins that `background` resolves to `var(--cream)` with the attribute on; (b) `.ap-btn`'s base selector is `.cmsg .approval .ap-btn` (**0-3-0**), so the attribute prefix ALONE (0-2-1) genuinely would have lost — this is the one place the brief's specificity hypothesis is real, and the compact rule carries the same three-class path (0-4-1). It also deliberately sets **no `color` and no `border-color`**, because those would erase a multi-select pick's gold `.on` state (Part 2). **`.cmsg .msg-act` (0-2-0) still outranks the bare-button rule**, so the per-message Copy/Edit/Fork/Delete row stays borderless text rather than becoming five boxes — asserted as a negative. **PART 2 — MULTI-SELECT ASK CARDS v2.** A `clarify` frame with `multi_select` no longer renders a hint that makes the user do the joining by hand: the option chips become **toggles** (gold outline when picked, via ONE new base rule `.cmsg .approval .ap-btn.on`, the same `#mode-browse.on` outline idiom — outline, never fill, so the confirm chip stays the card's only primary), plus a confirm chip reading **"answer with N selected"** that is dead until something is picked. **Picks are tracked by option INDEX, not label** — two choices may legitimately carry the same text and must toggle independently. The answer goes back as ONE string through the unchanged `POST /api/hermes/answer`: new PURE **`askJoin(labels)`** joins comma-separated, **except when a LABEL itself contains a comma, in which case it emits the JSON-array form** — upstream's `_parse_multi_select_response` (`vendor/hermes/tools/clarify_tool.py:86`, read this session) splits on "," unconditionally but also accepts `raw.startswith("[")` → `json.loads`, so a choice like "Speed, latency" would otherwise have been answered as two. Upstream strips each element, so the space after the comma is free. The collapsed card shows the LABELS (`{label: …}` → `esc(shown)`), never the JSON wire string. **Single-select cards, free text, cancel and the expire/freeze path are byte-unchanged**; the confirm is an ordinary `.ap-btn`, so `expireApprovals()` freezes it with everything else, and a `card._done` guard means a frozen card can be neither toggled nor confirmed. **TESTS: `test_compact_chrome.js` 55 → 75 checks, and the new ones FAIL on the shipped build** (verified by reverting the fix in a scratch copy: **8 failures**, of which the load-bearing three are the cascade deltas). The regression guard is a **real cascade resolver inside the test** — it parses the whole stylesheet (comment-stripped; the earlier naive version silently matched nothing because comment prose glues onto selectors), computes the winning declaration per property with the attribute off vs on, and DEMANDS a visible delta for a bare Mission-Control `<button>`, a `.cap-btn`, an `.ap-btn` and a lane chip. Greps prove a rule EXISTS; this proves it WINS and REACHES the screen — the exact gap that let this ship. Plus the two negatives above and "no compact rule matches while the attribute is absent". **ONE existing assertion changed HONESTLY:** the "does not restyle content elements" check tested `sel.includes(' .cmsg')`, which the new ap-btn rule trips on its ANCESTOR path; it now tests the **last compound** (what a rule TARGETS), which is strictly the right question and still forbids `.cmsg { }`. `test_hermes_sse_map.py` 61 → 78 checks (the multi-select wiring: frame-derived `multi`, toggle-not-answer, single-select untouched, index-keyed picks, the counted+disabled confirm, the freeze guard, the label-not-wire collapse, no-timer/no-auto-confirm negatives — plus **`askJoin` EXECUTED under node** on the shipped source: 6 cases incl. the comma-in-label JSON fallback, and null/undefined totality). **All 25 py + 17 js suites green, contract 76, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean, CSS balance 516/516 (was 510/510 — +6 rules: 5 in the sanctioned compact block, 1 base `.ap-btn.on`).** **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) **the compact axis now restyles the bare `<button>` element**, which is the broadest selector in the block by far — it is what makes the toggle mean anything on Mission Control, but it reaches every button in the panel at once, and any future control that wants to stay a text link must out-specify it the way `.msg-act` already does; (b) restating `background`/`border-color` on `button.primary` is a second home for the cream fill (the tie-break has no other honest fix short of `:not(.primary)` on the generic rule, which would have been a wider selector still); (c) `12px / 6px 13px / radius 6` and `.cap-btn` at 10.5px are builder numbers; (d) **one new BASE css rule** (`.ap-btn.on`) outside the compact block — the ask cards' zero-new-CSS record is broken by exactly one line, and the alternative was inline styles for a state the panel already has a class grammar for; (e) the JSON-array fallback in `askJoin` is a second wire form for the same answer (upstream supports both, but a reader of the feed will see `["a","b"]` in the bridge log while the card shows plain labels); (f) `multi_select` still renders as toggle CHIPS, not checkboxes — the chip is the panel's existing on/off grammar and checkboxes would need new CSS. **HONEST LIMITS: nothing here is run-verified in a browser** — the Chrome bridge was not connected this session, so the cascade claims come from a resolver written against the real stylesheet rather than from `getComputedStyle`, and the multi-select round trip needs a running Hermes with a model that calls `clarify` (the join contract is pinned at the vendored source and executed under node; the live answer is not). **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → on **Mission Control**, click **▣** → every card's **Start / Stop / Logs** button gains a solid ground with a tighter corner and the row reads as real buttons (this is the part that did nothing before) → click ▣ again → they go back to outlines, and the ◐ theme toggle still works independently in both states → Capabilities → the **Add server / Minimal preset** buttons change too → Chat → the lane chips fill gold as before. Then **HERMES** lane → ask something that makes it call `clarify` with several answers → the option chips now **toggle gold** and a **"answer with N selected"** chip appears → pick two, confirm → the card collapses to `✓ <both labels>` and the turn carries on. A single-answer question must still answer on ONE click.

> **👯 THE SAME TAB IN BOTH PANES — ON-DEMAND SECOND INSTANCES SHIPPED (2026-08-14h, Opus-5 builder to the Fable enhancement; `app/main.swift` + its grep test ONLY; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's ask — dragging a tab the OTHER pane already shows should be able to open a **SECOND INSTANCE** of that app here instead of always swapping. **THE SEPARATION IS THE DESIGN AND IT IS TEST-PINNED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS: the STRIP still swaps** (clicking it stays predictable — `routeTab` is byte-unchanged and a negative asserts `openSecondInstance` is unreachable from `routeTab`/`tabChanged`, appearing exactly twice in the file: its declaration and its ONE call site); **the DRAG is what gained the power.** `dropTab` now computes `other` (the tab the OTHER pane holds; with the split OFF that is the single pane itself) and, on a collision, calls `openSecondInstance(tab, onPane:)` instead of routing. **THE WHOLE STATE ADDITION IS TWO BOOLEANS + one lazily-keyed dictionary** — `leftIsGhost` / `rightIsGhost` / `secondInstances: [Int: WKWebView]`. A copy can only exist while both panes hold the SAME tab, and **the PRIMARY always lives in exactly one pane**, so "which pane holds the copy" is the only fact there is to remember; `applyPanes` **normalises the flags before anything reads them** (not the same tab ⇒ both false; both true ⇒ impossible, right cleared) and the placeholder survives as the safety net for the one state that is now neither a distinct tab nor a copy. **THE GHOST IS BUILT FROM THE PRIMARY'S OWN CONFIGURATION** (`WKWebView(frame:.zero, configuration: webViewFor(idx).configuration)` — the getter returns a COPY), which is what carries **the Odysseus skin user script** and the default website data store, so a second Odysseus is styled like the first and **shares its cookies/login rather than demanding a second sign-in**. ⚠️ it is a **plain WKWebView, deliberately NOT a DropWebView**: file-drop handling belongs to Mission Control's primary, and **the DropOverlay is now located by IDENTITY (`leftWV === panelWV`) rather than by tab index** — the old index test literally could not tell the panel's primary from a copy of it. That grep was updated honestly and is strictly stronger. **MEMORY DISCIPLINE IS ONE RULE, NOT FOUR CALL SITES:** `releaseUnusedGhosts()` runs inside `applyPanes` right after the flags are normalised and destroys anything the flags no longer claim — which covers **closing the split, closing either pane, and moving a pane to another tab** in one place. `destroyGhost` does `removeValue` → `stopLoading()` → delegates nil → `failedLoads.remove` → `removeFromSuperview()`, and the dictionary entry IS the only strong reference. **Ghosts are never parked** (the park loop iterates the five primaries only). **`urlFor(_:)` resolved webviews by IDENTITY and therefore could not answer for a copy** — it would have sent a ⌘R on a Hermes copy to the bridge's URL; both it and ghost creation now go through one new `urlForTab(_:)` table, and `visibleWebView()` returns the COPY when the focused pane is showing one, so **⌘R and the "Not reachable yet" retry work on a second instance exactly as on a primary.** **PERSISTENCE: nothing new, deliberately.** `persistTabs()` writes left == right and the EXISTING restore repair (`if rightTab == currentTab { rightTab = (currentTab + 1) % count }`) turns a relaunch back into the swap-based arrangement — ⚠️ **ghosts do not survive a relaunch, by spec**, and a negative test asserts no fifth `harness.split.*` key was invented. **HERMES CAVEAT, recorded not blocked:** two Hermes dashboards is upstream-supported (it is what two browser tabs do), **but the recorded WS limit still applies — do not DRIVE the same Hermes session from two surfaces at once**, because `prompt.submit`/`session.resume` rebind that session's transport to the caller (`methods_prompt.py:126-131`) and silently mute the other view. Two dashboards on DIFFERENT sessions is fine. ⚠️ also: `maybeReloadStaleHermes` still acts on the primary only (a copy is created fresh, so it cannot be stale at birth). **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) the split-OFF right-half drop of the tab the single pane already shows **no longer steps the left tab to `(tab+1)`** — it now opens the split with BOTH panes on that app, which is the literal ask but IS a changed behaviour (the old rule's string is asserted GONE); (b) **closing the pane that holds the PRIMARY keeps the primary and discards the copy**, so that copy's own navigation state is lost — the price of "the primary is never destroyed", and there is nowhere honest to put it once one pane remains; (c) **a ghost of the panel tab (idx 0) is allowed** — a second Mission Control talking to the same bridge, with its own singleton chat state; it cannot receive file drops (the overlay stays with the primary) and nothing stops two panels from both driving the same session, the same class of caveat as Hermes; (d) dragging the tab onto the pane that ALREADY holds the copy while the other holds the primary **swaps which pane has the copy** (harmless, visually identical, but it is motion for no reason); (e) `openSecondInstance` sets both `currentTab` and `rightTab` to the tab even though one assignment is always a no-op — deliberate, it makes the invariant readable rather than inferred. **TESTS: `test_audio_drop.js` 56 → 74 checks** (the two-booleans-plus-dict state, the colliding-drop branch, **the strip-still-swaps negative**, the old stepping rule asserted gone, the configuration copy, the not-a-DropWebView negative, `urlForTab` + `urlFor`'s ghost-first lookup, ⌘R on a copy, `destroyGhost`'s full teardown incl. the `failedLoads` cleanup, `Array(keys)` iteration, the keep-conditions, flags-normalised-before-release, the both-false/both-true rules, the park-only-primaries rule, the no-new-defaults-key negative, and the `ghost ->` slog lines). **FOUR existing assertions updated HONESTLY:** the DropOverlay grep (index → identity, see above) and **three purely mechanical distance widenings** inside `dropTab`'s greps (600→900, 800→1000, 1300→1600) because that body grew by the collision branch — the facts pinned are unchanged. **This also CLOSES the one pre-existing failure the max-turn session flagged** (*"a drop on the right half with the split OFF opens the split"*): it was that same distance, already stale in the dirty tree. **All 17 js + 25 py suites green, contract 76, ast OK; the panel was not touched at all** (CSS unchanged by construction). **HONEST LIMIT: still NO swiftc in the sandbox** — validated by a string-aware balance pass (**braces 297/297, parens 732/732, brackets 55/55, final depth 0, no negatives**) and careful reading; **`./scripts/ship.sh` IS the compile check**, and nothing about a second live WKWebView sharing a copied configuration, the cookie sharing, or the ghost teardown is run-verified. `applyPanes` changed by ~13 removed / ~28 added lines — inside the brief's ~40-line ceiling, no restructuring beyond it. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → click **⫽** → two panes → **drag the tab the OTHER pane is showing onto this pane → BOTH panes now show that app side by side** (try Hermes: two dashboards; try Odysseus: the second one is already logged in) → click **✕ on either pane** → back to one pane and **the copy is gone** (`log stream --predicate 'process == "Harness"'` shows `[split] ghost -> destroyed …`) → ⫽ again and repeat, then **click the STRIP on the tab the other pane holds → the two panes SWAP as before** (the strip's behaviour must be unchanged) → with the split OFF, **drag the current tab onto the right half → the split opens with the same app in both panes** → focus a pane showing a copy and press **⌘R** → that copy reloads, not the other pane → **relaunch** → the arrangement comes back as two DIFFERENT tabs (copies are not persisted, by design).

> **⏱️ HERMES MAX-TURN-TIME SERVER GUARD + SHIP.SH HARDENING SHIPPED (2026-08-14g, Opus-5 builder to the Fable spec; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** **TASK 1 — a runaway deliberation loop can no longer cook the CPU, and the fix stops the GENERATION rather than just the stream.** A 4B thinking model can spiral, and **every existing watchdog was structurally blind to it: they all measure SILENCE, and a spiralling model is not silent** — it emits continuously, so the 20s liveness tick never fires, the 60s first-event guard cannot apply, and the 600s hard guard (`silent >= 600.0`) never accumulates. The only bound was wall-clock exhaustion, and even when a guard DID fire it only ended **our relay** while Hermes kept generating. New **top-level `hermes.max_turn_s`** (default **600**, `0` = disabled) + the relay now measures **TOTAL turn time armed at `prompt.submit`**, checks it **on the EVENT path as well as on the timeout tick** (the event path is the only place a runaway loop is visible), and on overrun sends **`session.interrupt` itself** — the same RPC `/api/hermes/stop` uses — then yields `{"type":"proxy_error","error":"turn exceeded 600s — interrupted server-side (hermes.max_turn_s)"}` and ends. **THE CLOCK PAUSES WHILE AN INTERACTIVE CARD IS PENDING** and that is the single most important property: `approval_pending` (which already covers BOTH `approval.request` and `clarify.request`) opens a pause interval, resolving it closes one, and `hermes_turn_spent` subtracts both the closed total and the still-open interval — **a card waiting for Debi can never be shot for being slow**, no matter how long she takes (unit-pinned: a turn paused at second 1 still reads 1s spent after 10 000s). **THE CONFIG READ FAILS TOWARD THE GUARD EXISTING:** absent, `null`, junk, a list, NaN/inf, or even `True` all resolve to the 600s default — **only a deliberate `0`/negative disables it**, because a typo must not silently switch off a safety guard; and the value is read **ONCE at submit** so a mid-turn config edit cannot retune a live turn (the same rule the voice hangover read follows). A yaml hiccup at read time degrades to the default instead of killing the turn. **⚠️ THE KEY IS TOP-LEVEL `hermes:`, DELIBERATELY NOT `components.hermes.max_turn_s`, AND THAT IS A SHIPPING FACT NOT A STYLE CHOICE: `ship.sh`'s manifest merge is additive at the TOP LEVEL ONLY** (`for k, v in src.items(): if k not in dst`) — `components.hermes` already exists in every provisioned snapshot, so a new SUB-key under it would **never reach the app** and the guard would read its default forever while the yaml claimed otherwise. **NOTHING PRE-EXISTING WAS TOUCHED:** the 60s first-event guard, the 600s total-silence guard (still the right tool for the relay-dead case), the 20s `hermes_ping` heartbeat, the fail-safe `session.active_list` probe and the whole panel-Stop path are all asserted intact by negative tests. The overrun check is placed **BEFORE** the two silence guards on the tick, so when two come due on the same tick the one that also stops Hermes wins; and **AFTER** the `action == "done"` break, so a turn that completed on its final event can never be reported as an overrun. `_hermes_kill_turn` logs `[hermes] turn exceeded max_turn_s (Ns >= Ns) — sending session.interrupt` **plus the RPC's own outcome** to the bridge log (so the next diagnosis does not depend on the panel having been open) and is best-effort by construction — an unreachable gateway still ends the stream. **TASK 2 — `ship.sh` can no longer print a false green.** The wait loop was **30s and then FELL THROUGH**, printing `[ship] bridge is up` whether or not anything was listening; the fingerprint then hashed an **empty** curl body to `da39a3ee` and presented it as a successful ship. Now: **90s** (a cold snapshot bridge legitimately exceeds 30), an explicit `UP` flag, and on timeout `[ship] BRIDGE DID NOT COME UP within 90s — check data/logs/bridge.log (tail below)` + `tail -15` of the **snapshot's** bridge.log + **`exit 1`**; an empty openapi body prints `fingerprint unavailable (bridge still starting?)` instead of hashing emptiness. **Every other ship.sh behaviour is byte-identical and negative-tested: the additive manifest merge, the None→empty-scalar representer, the `.git` lock clears, the newer-than-binary recompile rule, the listener-scoped port kill, and never copying harness.yaml wholesale.** **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) **the guard's "feed-able line" is emitted as an extra `hermes_status` frame** immediately before the `proxy_error` (so the panel's existing statusline shows the reason) rather than by adding a `feed()` call — **the panel was NOT edited at all** in this slice, which keeps it reversible, but it does mean the overrun does not appear in the activity feed proper; (b) **the tick-path check has ≤20s granularity** (it rides the existing timeout, no new timer) — irrelevant against a 600s budget, visible if Debi sets `max_turn_s: 15`; (c) **the pause is keyed on `approval_pending`, i.e. exactly the two card events**, so any FUTURE blocking prompt type would need adding there or it would burn budget while waiting for a human; (d) `600` as the default is the spec's number and matches the old hard guard, so the guard's arrival changes nothing for a healthy long turn; (e) the overrun ends the turn even if `session.interrupt` fails (correct — a wedged gateway is exactly when the stream must close — but it does mean the message can claim "interrupted" when the RPC did not land; the bridge log distinguishes the two); (f) `SHIP_WAIT_S=90` and `tail -15` are the spec's numbers. **TESTS: new `bridge/tests/test_hermes_max_turn.py` — 83 checks** (the full config-read totality table incl. `True`/NaN/inf/list/dict/`None`-config and a negative that `components.hermes.max_turn_s` is NOT read; the clock incl. clock-skew flooring, closed+open pauses, a pause longer than the turn, a future-dated pause and the never-accrues invariant; the boundary-inclusive overrun decision with junk-tolerance; the exact wording; then the WIRING — budget read exactly once, both check sites, both calling the interrupt, the tick check ordered before both silence guards, the event check ordered after the done-break, the pause open/close bookkeeping, `_hermes_kill_turn` using `session.interrupt` and never raising, the live `harness.yaml` parsed and read back as 600 — plus every ship.sh assertion above, positive and negative). **All 25 py + 17 js suites green, contract 76 unchanged, ast OK, `bash -n` clean on ship.sh.** **⚠️ ONE PRE-EXISTING FAILURE, NOT FROM THIS SLICE: `bridge/tests/test_audio_drop.js` fails one assertion** — *"a drop on the right half with the split OFF opens the split"*. `app/main.swift` and that test file were **already dirty in the tree** when this session started (the uncommitted drag-a-tab slice); neither was touched here, and the failure reproduces on the tree as found. It belongs to the drag-tab slice's own QA. **HONEST LIMIT: the guard cannot be exercised in the sandbox** — it needs a running gateway and a model that actually spirals; the pure half is unit-tested, the wiring is grep-pinned, and the live interrupt round trip is read out of the same RPC `/api/hermes/stop` already uses in production. **Files: `bridge/app.py`** (the block before `_hermes_session_working` ≈L4146: `HERMES_MAX_TURN_S_DEFAULT`, pure `hermes_max_turn_s`/`hermes_turn_spent`/`hermes_turn_overrun`/`hermes_overrun_error`, `_hermes_kill_turn`; then inside `hermes_chat`'s relay: the arm-at-submit block ≈L4325, the tick check ≈L4360, the pause bookkeeping ≈L4415, the event-path check ≈L4445), **`harness.yaml`** (new top-level `hermes:` block above `memory:`), **`scripts/ship.sh`** (the wait/fingerprint tail), new **`bridge/tests/test_hermes_max_turn.py`**. No vendor edits, no panel edit, no commit. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → **it should now say `bridge is up` only when it really is**, and if the bridge fails to start you get the log tail and a non-zero exit instead of a false green. Then **Chat → HERMES** lane → send something that makes the small model spiral (the long-thinking prompts that used to hang) → **within ~10 minutes the turn ENDS by itself** with `[proxy error: turn exceeded 600s — interrupted server-side (hermes.max_turn_s)]`, the fans wind down (Hermes actually stopped, not just the panel), and `data/logs/bridge.log` carries the `[hermes] turn exceeded max_turn_s …` line. To test it quickly, set `max_turn_s: 30` in the SNAPSHOT's `harness.yaml` (`~/Library/Application Support/Harness/harness.yaml` — no restart needed, it is read per turn) and send any prompt: the turn should die at ~30s. Then send one that raises an **approval or clarify card** and **leave it sitting for well over 30s before answering** → **the card must NOT be killed**, and the turn must complete normally after you answer. Put it back to 600 afterwards.

> **▣ OPTIONAL "COMPACT" CHROME SHIPPED — DEBI'S LM-STUDIO BUTTON VISION, STRICTLY OPT-IN (2026-08-14f, Opus-5 builder to the Fable design; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** the queued "deeper theme with real button UI" (Debi 2026-08-13) is built as a **THIRD theme axis, INDEPENDENT of light/dark**: `html[data-chrome="compact"]`. It changes **CONTROL DENSITY AND SHAPE ONLY** — no palette token, no content typography, no editorial serif header, no layout. The small-caps mono **text-chips become real buttons**: solid `var(--card2)` ground, 1px `var(--line2)` border, 6px radius, tighter padding, `letter-spacing:normal`, `text-transform:none` (so labels read in the Title case they are already authored in — `Agent` / `Chat` / `Hermes` / `Eject` / `Switch` all already are), hover = `var(--gold)` border on `var(--card)`, active = **filled gold**. Icon-only chips (◐ ▣ ⌘K ↻ ⊕) become **26px squares with the glyph centred**. `button.primary` reads firmer (8px radius, explicit 1px border). **THE WHOLE POINT IS THAT IT CAN BE OFF AND THEN NOTHING CHANGED, and that is enforced structurally, not by care: EXACTLY 18 RULES, every one prefixed `html[data-chrome="compact"]`, in ONE delimited block that is the LAST content in the stylesheet** (last = it wins on equal specificity without any `!important`) — **and a test asserts the string `data-chrome` appears NOWHERE ELSE in the stylesheet**, so with the attribute absent the panel is byte-for-byte today's editorial chrome. This is the sanctioned exception to the zero-new-rules discipline: **CSS 492 → 510 braces, +18 rules, balanced** (note: the "500/500" figure carried in earlier memory counted differently — the pre-slice tree measures 492/492 by the `<style>`-block brace count used here, and HEAD+18 = 510 exactly). **TOGGLE = ITS OWN CHIP `▣` BESIDE `◐`, PLUS ⌘K "Toggle compact chrome" ⚠️ (the brief offered either; this is the pick, and it is deliberate):** folding a second axis into `◐`'s cycle would make **four states reachable only by repeated clicking** — a click on the theme chip would stop being deterministic, and the two axes are genuinely orthogonal (compact dark and compact light are both wanted). `toggleTheme()` and `toggleChrome()` are asserted by test to **touch neither the other's attribute nor the other's storage key**. Persisted as `localStorage['harness-chrome']` and applied **in the SAME pre-paint `<head>` script as the theme**, so controls never reflow after first paint; OFF **removes the attribute entirely** rather than writing a second value. The flip logs one activity-feed line. **ONE THING THE EDITORIAL CHIPS GET WRONG AND THIS BLOCK DOES NOT: gold-fill ink.** The existing `.mode-chip.on` / `.mp-act.on` hardcode `#171420`, which is dark ink chosen for the dark palette; in **light** theme `--gold` is `#9c7327` (dark) so dark-on-dark would be the result. The compact block uses **`var(--bg)`** as its fill ink, which is correct in BOTH themes — asserted by a negative test that `#171420` never appears in the block. (The existing rules were NOT changed: out of slice.) **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) **the font stack change is the one thing that is not purely geometric** — chrome text drops `var(--mono)` for a `-apple-system … system-ui` stack (factored into a single `--chrome-font` var defined only inside this axis, the ONE custom property the block declares); it is what makes a chip read as a button rather than a label, but it IS a typographic change to chrome and Fable may want it reverted to mono-at-normal-tracking; (b) **sizes are scaled PROPORTIONALLY, not flattened to one value** — Fable's spec named 11.5px, applied here to `.chip`; the composer chips are 9-10.5px and the `talk/auto/conv` micro-chips 9.5px, because 11.5px on a 7px control would tear the composer row apart (`#chat-send` gains a little padding to match); (c) **`Browse` stays OUTLINE-only when on** (`var(--card2)` ground, gold border, gold text) rather than filled — that is the existing editorial restraint rule (filled gold is reserved for the active LANE, a capability must whisper) re-expressed in button shape, but it means one control's "on" looks different from the others'; (d) **scope is literally what Fable named** — `.chip` (and therefore `.caps-tab`), `.chip-icon`, `.mp-act`, `.mode-chip`, the two closed pickers, the voice micro-chips, `button.primary`; **`.cap-btn`, `.cap-exp`, `.art-btn`, `.ap-btn` and the session-rail controls are deliberately NOT touched**, so Capabilities' own small buttons and the approval-card chips keep the editorial look while the chrome around them turns button-y — consistent-by-omission is a judgement call and the easiest thing to extend later; (e) `button` generally is untouched (only `button.primary`), so non-primary buttons keep their 9px radius against the chips' 6px; (f) the chip glyph `▣` and the wording "compact chrome" are builder choices; (g) the chip's own "on" look lives **inside** the compact block (it can only be on when the axis is on), which is why zero rules were added outside it. **TESTS: new `bridge/tests/test_compact_chrome.js` — 57 checks**, and they are mostly NEGATIVES because that is where the risk is: the block exists and is the last content in the stylesheet, the rule count is **read out of the panel** (a 19th rule trips this file rather than passing silently), every selector part carries the prefix, `data-chrome` appears nowhere else, the block declares exactly one custom property and **redefines no palette token**, it restyles no content element (`h1`/`.brand`/`.cmsg`/`.body`/`body`), all eight named targets are covered, `#171420` is absent, the pre-paint script applies theme-then-chrome in one pass, OFF removes the attribute, the choice is persisted and fed, **each toggle ignores the other axis**, and both entry points exist with the chip sitting after `◐`. **All 24 py + 17 js suites green, contract 76, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** Built ON TOP of the uncommitted toolset-lever (`bridge/app.py` + panel) and drag-tab (`app/main.swift`) slices; **nothing reverted**. **HONEST LIMIT: not seen rendered** — a CSS density pass cannot be judged headlessly, and the 26px icon squares / composer row widths / gold-fill contrast in **light** theme all want Debi's eyes. **Files: `bridge/panel/index.html`** — CSS block L883-956 (18 rules, at the end of the stylesheet), pre-paint L960, chip L989, `toggleChrome()` L1820, palette command L9117 — and new `bridge/tests/test_compact_chrome.js`. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → top-right, a new **`▣`** chip sits beside **`◐`** → click it → **every chip in the app becomes a bordered button** (topbar squares, Models/Capabilities sub-tabs with the active one filled gold, the composer's Agent/Chat/Hermes lanes, the model + AUDIO pickers, the popover Eject/Switch actions) while **all the type, colour and layout stay exactly as they were** → click **◐** → light theme in compact chrome, and the filled-gold active chips are still readable → click **▣** again → **everything returns to today's editorial look exactly** → reload the app → the choice survived with **no flash of the other style**. Also ⌘K → **"Toggle compact chrome"** does the same thing.

> **✂️ HERMES TOOLSET TRIMMING LEVER SHIPPED — THE ~19k PREFILL IS NOW A SWITCH (2026-08-14e, Opus-5 builder to the Fable slice; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's Hermes lane hands the local 4B **35 tools + 78 skills ≈ 17-19k tokens of system prefill** before a single token comes back — minutes per turn. There is now a **Capabilities → Tools → "Hermes tools"** group: one switch per toolset, a **Minimal preset** chip (file + terminal + clarify) and **Everything back on**, with the real tool counts on every row and `N/M toolsets on · X of Y tools in the prompt` at the top. **THE MECHANISM WAS READ OUT OF THE PIN, NOT GUESSED, AND THE OBVIOUS KEYS DO NOT EXIST: there is no `tools.disabled_toolsets`, no `tools.enabled_toolsets`, no `toolsets.enabled`, no `agent.tools`** (the top-level `toolsets:` key DOES exist, default `["hermes-cli"]`, `hermes_cli/config_defaults.py:12` — but it is **vestigial**: only `tools/kanban_tools.py:59` and `hermes_cli/dump.py` read it, and it does NOT drive the tool list). **The real key is `platform_toolsets.cli`.** Our lane is the dashboard's TUI gateway, which resolves the model's toolsets in `_load_enabled_toolsets()` (**tui_gateway/server.py:3779**), consumed per session build at **server.py:6163**, via `_get_platform_tools(cfg, "cli", include_default_mcp_servers=True)` (**server.py:3910**) — and that function reads `config.get("platform_toolsets").get(platform)` (**hermes_cli/tools_config.py:2195-2205**). A saved list is **authoritative** (`has_explicit_config` → direct membership, **tools_config.py:2226-2240**), so it is not re-expanded from the `hermes-cli` composite. **NO RESTART IS NEEDED, and that is proven not assumed:** `_get_platform_tools` has **no memoisation**, `load_config()` is **mtime+size keyed** (`hermes_cli/config.py:3105`), and `get_tool_definitions`'s own memo key **includes the config mtime** (`model_tools.py:320-336`) — so an edit lands on the **NEXT CHAT**. The endpoint reports `restart_required:false` rather than making the panel assume. **THE SKILLS TOOLSET IS THE SINGLE BIGGEST LEVER and the row says so:** upstream gates the ENTIRE `<available_skills>` block on `skills_list`/`skill_view`/`skill_manage` being in the schema (`has_skills_tools`, **agent/system_prompt.py:299**, `else: skills_prompt = ""` :326) — so switching `skills` off drops 3 tool schemas **AND all 78 skills** in one click (the row reads "3 tools + the whole skill index (78 skills)", count probed live from `GET /api/skills`). **WRITES GO THROUGH HERMES'S OWN WRITER — deliberately NOT our yaml round-trip, which is a DEVIATION FROM THE BRIEF (⚠️ Fable's call):** `PUT /api/tools/toolsets/{name}` (**hermes_cli/web_routers/tools.py:113**) does load_config → `_get_platform_tools(…, include_default_mcp_servers=False)` → add/discard → `_save_platform_tools` (**tools_config.py:2491**) → `save_config` (`strip_defaults=True`, `atomic_yaml_write`). That helper is the only thing that knows to **preserve MCP-server names parked in the same list**, to route platform-restricted toolsets (discord → `platform_toolsets.discord`), and to keep the `known_builtin_toolsets`/`known_plugin_toolsets` bookkeeping coherent; reimplementing it would be a **SECOND writer of the same key that could drift at any pin bump**. The price is that WRITING needs Hermes **running** — the same precedent the voice-MCP rows already set (a switch that cannot work is DISABLED, not silently ineffective), and the catalog is likewise **PROBED** (`GET /api/tools/toolsets`) rather than hardcoded, so a pin bump that adds or renames a toolset cannot leave a stale table in our source. **THE FOOTGUN THAT SHAPED THE GUARD: `platform_toolsets.cli: []` means ALL TOOLSETS, not none.** With an empty list `has_explicit_config` is False, the else-branch expands nothing, and `_load_enabled_toolsets` turns an empty result into **`return None`** (**server.py:3915-3917**) which `get_tool_definitions` reads as "no restriction" — so turning the LAST switch off would hand the model MORE tools than it had. The bridge therefore **refuses** any desired set with no known toolset in it (400, with the reason spelled out), and the refusal is asserted to run BEFORE any write. **THREE MORE HONEST EDGES, all surfaced rather than swallowed:** (1) **`agent.disabled_toolsets`** (default `[]`, `config_defaults.py:228`) is subtracted **LAST** (`tools_config.py:2455-2463`), so a name parked there can never be enabled from this panel — the row is pilled **`forced off in config`**, and a POST re-probes and returns `stuck:[…]` naming that key instead of letting a switch snap back; (2) **`agent.coding_context: "focus"`** (default `"auto"`, `config_defaults.py:131`) makes `coding_selection()` return its own list and `_load_enabled_toolsets` **RETURN EARLY** (`server.py:3797-3803`; focus-only per > **✅ FABLE QA: CLARIFY CARDS APPROVED (2026-08-14, Fable 5; Debi-verified live — multi-round asks working):** all 6 tags approved — label-not-index answering is the right anti-staleness design; multi_select hint over checkboxes correct for v1; unconditional allows_free_text accepted (upstream's rule); wording/constants/inline-input accepted.

> **🫳 DRAG A TAB ONTO A PANE SHIPPED (2026-08-14d, Opus-5 builder to the Fable split-view-v2 enhancement; `app/main.swift` ONLY; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** the queued half of split-view v2 — **drag a tab LABEL out of the strip and drop it on a pane**; the pane takes that tab, and dropping on the right half when the split is OFF **opens the split**. **THE IMPLEMENTATION SHAPE WAS FORCED, NOT CHOSEN, AND THIS IS THE ONE FACT THE NEXT READER NEEDS: `NSSegmentedControl` exposes no per-segment drag source AND it handles a click inside a CELL TRACKING LOOP that pulls events straight off the queue — so a local monitor watching for `.leftMouseDragged` after the mouseDown would NEVER BE CALLED.** The shell therefore **claims the mouseDown itself** (a second `NSEvent.addLocalMonitorForEvents([.leftMouseDown])`, deliberately separate from the focus `clickMonitor` — a strip click is in NEITHER pane, so the two can never fight over one event) and runs the whole gesture in its own `NSApp.nextEvent(matching: [.leftMouseDragged, .leftMouseUp, .keyDown], inMode: .eventTracking)` loop. Below a **10pt** movement threshold the gesture IS the ordinary click and is performed verbatim (`seg.selectedSegment = tab; tabChanged(seg)`); above it, the drag engages. Any mouseDown that does not resolve to a segment (the **⫽** button, bare strip, anywhere else) is **returned untouched** and behaves exactly as today. **THE GEOMETRY HAD TO BE MADE KNOWABLE: `width(forSegment:)` returns 0 for an auto-sized segment, i.e. with autosizing the strip's layout is UNREADABLE** — and since the shell now owns the CLICK too, a wrong hit test would break plain tab switching, not just the new gesture. So `setSegmentWidths()` sets each width to measured-text + 26pt at construction and `segmentAt()` walks those same numbers, with the bounds/sum slack inset at the two ends (or scaled if it is large) so a bezel inset cannot accumulate into a systematic offset. **ONE REAL STRUCTURAL WIN, not a side effect: the swap rule moved out of `tabChanged` into `routeTab(_ idx:toPane:)`** — the destination pane is now a PARAMETER, the strip passes the focused pane and a drop passes the pane it landed on, so **the click path and the drop path are ONE body and cannot drift** (`tabChanged` is now two lines over it; behaviour byte-identical). Drop resolution: split ON → the REAL pane frames decide (a dragged divider is honoured), split OFF → the geometric halves of the content area, which is exactly what the drop will create. `dropTab` split-ON = `routeTab` + `setFocus(pane)`; split-OFF right-half = open the split with the dragged tab on the right — and ⚠️ **dropping the tab the single pane ALREADY shows onto the right half has no sibling to swap with, so the tab MOVES right and the left takes `(tab+1) % count`, the same step ⫽ itself makes.** State rides the **existing four keys** (a test NEGATIVE pins that no `harness.split.drag` key was invented), and `maybeReloadStaleHermes` fires on the split-open path too, so a backgrounded Hermes dashboard still gets its reload as it reappears. **VISUALS: a translucent gold-bordered label following the cursor + a gold rect over the pane that would receive it — both as `ignoresMouseEvents` borderless CHILD WINDOWS, ⚠️ deliberately not overlay NSViews:** the container is an autolayout hierarchy (a frame-positioned subview would be re-laid-out from under us mid-drag) and an extra subview of a pane HOST would also have to be reasoned about in `applyPanes`, which asserts what a host's LAST subview is for the DropOverlay. Both are framed BEFORE being added as children (no flash at screen origin) and torn down through a single `defer` on every exit path. **CANCEL: release over the strip / titlebar (anywhere outside the content rect), or Esc** — ⚠️ a keyDown may not be delivered at all while a button is held, so **the release-over-the-strip cancel is the one that always works**; plus a **60s per-event ceiling** so a lost mouseUp can never wedge the strip. Diagnostics kept in the house idiom: `[split] drag -> begin <title>`, `drag -> tab N to pane P`, `drag -> opened split: left=… right=…`, `drag -> cancelled (…)`. **NOTHING ELSE TOUCHED** — `buildPane`/`setFocus`/`applyPanes`/`closePane`/`positionDivider`/the ⫽ button/`DropOverlay`/`DownloadHandler` are all unchanged, and **ZERO new types** were added (the floaters are windows, not a new NSView subclass). **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) **the shell now owns strip clicks**, so a segment no longer draws its pressed highlight while the mouse is down (selection + action are identical) — the cost of the only structure that can see a drag at all; (b) **segment widths are now explicit**, a small rendered change (measured-text + 26pt instead of AppKit's autosize; ~450pt total for the five titles, so it cannot collide with ⫽ at the 900pt minimum window width); (c) the 10pt threshold, the 60s event ceiling and the 26pt/22pt paddings are builder numbers; (d) the same-tab-dropped-right rule in (⚠️) above; (e) the drag is strip-only — dragging a pane's CONTENT does nothing, by design; (f) the hint highlights the whole target pane rather than a half-pane "insert here" edge, because a drop assigns a pane, it does not create a third one. **TESTS: `test_audio_drop.js` 40 → 56 checks** (the monitor claiming only a resolvable segment, the focus monitor asserted still separate, explicit widths asserted to be set at construction, `segmentAt` returning nil off the strip, the sub-threshold click performed verbatim, the own tracking loop, the 60s ceiling, both cancels, the pane-vs-half target rule, both drop branches, the shared `routeTab`, the no-new-key negative, the mouse-transparent child windows + the single `defer` teardown, and the drag slog lines). **ONE existing assertion updated HONESTLY:** the old grep pinned the swap INSIDE `func tabChanged`; the swap genuinely moved to `routeTab`, and the replacement is strictly stronger — it pins the swap in `routeTab` AND, separately, that `tabChanged` still routes to the FOCUSED pane. **All 23 py + 15 js suites green, contract 65, ast OK.** **HONEST LIMIT: still NO swiftc in the sandbox** — validated by a string-aware balance pass (**braces 276/276, parens 698/698, brackets 49/49, final depth 0, no negatives**) and careful reading; **`./scripts/ship.sh` IS the compile check**, and nothing about the tracking loop, the child-window ghost or the segment hit-test arithmetic is run-verified. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → **first click tabs normally** (every tab, split off and on — this is the regression that matters most) → then **press on a tab label and drag**: a small gold-edged label follows the cursor and the half you are over lights up gold → **release on the RIGHT half → the window splits** with that tab on the right → drag another tab onto the LEFT pane → the left takes it → drag the tab the OTHER pane is holding onto this pane → **the two panes SWAP** → start a drag and release it back **on the tab strip** → nothing happens → relaunch → the arrangement comes back. If a drag ever picks the wrong tab, the floating label says which one it thinks it has — paste the `[split] drag -> …` lines (`log stream --predicate 'process == "Harness"'`).

> **❓ INTERACTIVE CLARIFY/ASK CARDS SHIPPED — THE HERMES LANE CAN NOW BE ASKED A QUESTION (2026-08-14c, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi proved it with screenshots: prompt **"test"** → the model calls Hermes's **`clarify`** tool → Hermes's OWN dashboard renders a picker (*"What should I test? 1. Functionality 2. Performance 3. Integration 4. Other (type your answer) · ↑/↓ select · Enter confirm · Esc cancel"*) — and **our panel had no surface for it at all**, so the turn sat "working" invisibly until something timed out or was interrupted. **Exactly the class approval cards already solved**, so it is built to the same shape. **PROTOCOL PINNED BY READING, NOT GUESSED (file:line):** `clarify` blocks the agent thread on a gateway prompt — `tui_gateway/server.py:5376-5390` `clarify_callback` → `_block("clarify.request", sid, {"question": q, "choices": c[, "multi_select": True]})`; **`multi_select` is emitted ONLY when true** (upstream's own comment, :5379-5387, says a single answer still parses as a one-element list). **UNLIKE approvals, a clarify IS addressed by id** — `_block` mints one and stamps `payload["request_id"] = rid` (**server.py:2856-2862**), so multiple asks in one turn are unambiguous and there is no FIFO guessing. A timed-out prompt emits **`<event>.expire` with `{request_id}`** — built, never written literally, as `f"{event.removesuffix('.request')}.expire"` (**server.py:2886-2896**) for the four blocking bridges whose respond tolerates a late reply. The answer goes back as **`clarify.respond` → `_respond(rid, params, "answer", allow_expired=True)`** (**methods_prompt.py:836-842**), which resolves on **`params["request_id"]` alone — no session_id anywhere** — and returns **`{"status":"ok"}` or `{"status":"expired"}`** (**server.py:9981-9992**). **The answer is a PLAIN STRING**, returned verbatim to the model as `user_response` (**tools/clarify_tool.py:170**) — there is **no index protocol on the wire**. **FREE TEXT IS ALWAYS AVAILABLE and that is upstream's rule, not our inference:** every renderer appends *"Other (type your answer)"* (**clarify_tool.py:22**, schema :233) and a choice-less clarify is open-ended by construction (:156-157). Also confirmed: clarify's tool lifecycle events are **never suppressed** even with tool progress off (`_tool_lifecycle_required_for_ui` **server.py:3944-3949**), and **`session.interrupt` releases the pending prompt scoped to that session** (`_clear_pending(sid)`, **methods_session.py:2723**) — which is why **panel Stop still works with a card open**. **BUILT — bridge (`bridge/app.py`):** the pure mapper gains `clarify.request` → SSE `{"type":"ask","request":{request_id, question, options[], multi_select, allows_free_text}}` (turn KEEPS streaming) and `clarify.expire` → `{"type":"ask_expire","request_id"}`; hostile payloads can never invent options (a STRING `choices` yields `[]`, never a split; blanks dropped; empty payload safe). The relay's `approval_pending` gate now also arms on `clarify.request`, so a pending ask **suppresses the 20s working-note AND the `active_list` liveness probe** exactly like an approval — a card that legitimately waits minutes must never read as a dead turn (the 20s `hermes_ping` heartbeat still flows, so the panel's own stall watchdog is unaffected, and the 600s hard guard is untouched). New **`POST /api/hermes/answer {request_id, answer|cancel, session_id?}`** → `clarify.respond {request_id, answer}`; 400 on a missing request_id / non-string / empty / >4000-char answer, 502 on RPC failure, and **`status:"expired"` is surfaced rather than swallowed** so the panel stamps honestly instead of claiming an answer that never reached the agent. **`cancel:true` sends the empty string — which is upstream's OWN skip** (`_clear_pending` resolves to `""`), so the agent gets a "no answer" it already knows how to handle. **PANEL (`bridge/panel/index.html`):** `chatAsk()` renders an inline card that **reuses the `.approval` CSS wholesale — ZERO new rules, CSS 500/500 unchanged** — question in the existing `.ap-cmd` mono block (expand/collapse past 600 chars), **one `.ap-btn` chip per option**, a faint `cancel` chip in the `deny` style, and a **free-text input + send** (Enter submits) in the existing inline-style idiom. The card carries the `.approval` class, so **`expireApprovals()` freezes ask cards on turn end for free — one freeze path, not two that can drift**. Answering disables everything instantly, then collapses to `✓ <chosen>` / `✗ cancelled`; a failure re-arms with an inline error (the prompt is still pending gateway-side); `ask_expire` stamps **only the card owning that request_id**. **Multiple asks per turn supported.** `turnStage('awaiting an answer')` labels the stage so a force-end says where it died. **CONV MODE: nothing to do, and that is the point** — an ask emits no conv event, the turn never ends, so the mic stays gated and the reply is not spoken; a test NEGATIVE pins that the ask path contains no `sendChat`/`convSend`/`setTimeout`, i.e. **nothing can ever answer on the user's behalf**. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) **there is no `choice_index` on our endpoint** though the brief offered one — resolving an index would mean the BRIDGE keeping a copy of the option list, duplicated gateway state whose staleness could answer a different question than the card on screen shows; the panel holds what it rendered and sends the LABEL, so what is answered is what was displayed; (b) **`multi_select` is not rendered as checkboxes in v1** — shown as a faint hint ("pick one, or type them comma-separated") because a single pick parses as a one-element list upstream and the free-text box already feeds `_parse_multi_select_response`; (c) `allows_free_text` is emitted as an **unconditional true** (upstream's rule, but it is a constant on our wire — a future renderer-side opt-out would need a real signal); (d) the card reuses `.ap-label`'s "⚠" glyph with the wording **"answer needed"** beside approvals' "approval required" — same register, deliberately; (e) `ASK_ANSWER_MAX = 4000` and `ASK_Q_TRUNC = 600` are builder numbers; (f) the free-text input is styled **inline** rather than with a new class (the zero-new-CSS rule), so it is the one control on the card without a stylesheet home. **TESTS: `test_hermes_sse_map.py` 37 → 61 checks** (the full ask/expire mapper table incl. the string-choices negative, blank-dropping, the never-ends-a-turn rule and the unconditional free-text rule, plus wiring greps for the endpoint, the request_id addressing, the cancel semantics, the expired surfacing, the watchdog suppression, the panel's two frame handlers, the shared `.approval` freeze path and the no-auto-answer negative) and **contract `test_clarify_protocol_contract`** pinning every fact above at the vendored source — the emit shape, the `request_id` stamp, the `.expire` construction (asserted structurally, since upstream never writes that literal), `clarify.respond`'s answer key + `allow_expired`, the expired status, the verbatim `user_response`, the always-appended free-text option, and `session.interrupt`'s `_clear_pending`. **All 23 py + 15 js suites green, contract 65 (was 64), CSS 500/500 with ZERO new rules, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** **HONEST LIMIT: the live path cannot be exercised in the sandbox** (it needs a running Hermes gateway and a model that actually calls `clarify`) — the protocol is read out of the vendored source and the mapper/wiring are unit-tested, but the round trip is not run-verified. Built ON TOP of the uncommitted turn-lifecycle/interrupt fix already in the tree; nothing reverted. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → **HERMES** lane → send **"test"** → **a card appears** reading *answer needed* with the model's question, one chip per option, a `cancel` chip and a *"or type your answer…"* box → click an option → it collapses to `✓ <that option>` **and the turn carries on and completes**. Repeat and instead **type an answer** and press Enter → same. Repeat and click **cancel** → `✗ cancelled` and the turn continues. Finally start another one and click **Stop** while the card is open → the turn ends and the card stamps `· expired`.

> **🩺 HERMES-LANE "OPERATION INTERRUPTED" REGRESSION — CONVICTED + FIXED (2026-08-14b, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi: the Hermes lane worked on **2026-08-06** (same Parable-Qwen3-4B, a real tool-use turn completed); TODAY the prompt "test" showed `thinking…` / `mulling…` + the bridge's 20s prefill note and then, after ~6-7 minutes, the reply came back as **"Operation interrupted."** — with no Stop clicked. The direct lane was fine at 73 tok/s and Hermes is still v2026.7.30, so neither the model nor the runner nor an upstream bump is the suspect. **THE STRING IS THE EVIDENCE: "Operation interrupted." is HERMES's own text** (`vendor/hermes/agent/message_sanitization.py:310` — an interrupted turn whose streamed text is empty is persisted as exactly that; `conversation_loop.py:3338-3368` is the `except InterruptedError` that produces it). So the turn did not stall — it was **genuinely interrupted upstream**. **WHO CAN DO THAT:** on the gateway side only two seams raise it — `session.interrupt` (`tui_gateway/methods_session.py:2745`) and a *second* `prompt.submit` on a busy session (`tui_gateway/server.py:7127-7152` → `_interrupt_busy_session`). Our stack calls `session.interrupt` in exactly ONE place (`bridge/app.py:4319`, reachable only via `POST /api/hermes/stop`), which the panel reaches only through `hermesStop()` — and `hermesStop()` has exactly five callers: the Send-becomes-Stop branch and **the four escape hatches `stopTurnNow()` was wired into on 2026-08-14 (`bc164ec`)**. **THE REGRESSION IS `bc164ec` ITSELF: it converted four `if (chatBusy) return;` no-ops into turn-killers, and three of those paths are reachable with NO intent to stop anything.** (1) **`initChat()` is the damning one** — `showView('chat')` calls it whenever no Odysseus session is bound (`index.html:1762`), which is the NORMAL state of the Hermes lane (that lane needs no Odysseus session, and Odysseus is often stopped), and in Hermes mode `initChat` calls `selectHermesSession(hermesStoredSid)` — i.e. **merely leaving the Chat view and coming back mid-turn now fired `session.interrupt`**, then cleared `#chat-msgs` and rebuilt the pane from `/api/hermes/session/resume`, which is precisely why the reply reads as Hermes's stored "Operation interrupted." rather than our own `· interrupted` stamp. (2) **clicking the rail row of the session you are already in** — the easiest thing in the panel to click while waiting six minutes — did the same. (3) **the lane guards ran AFTER the stop** (`selectHermesSession`'s `if (chatMode !== 'hermes') return;` and `selectSession`'s mirror), so a cross-lane call that was about to do nothing at all still killed the live turn first. On 2026-08-06 every one of these was an inert `return`, which is exactly why the same slow turn completed then. **The ~6-7 minutes is not a timer — it is when Debi interacted.** **RULED OUT WITH EVIDENCE, not assumed:** the panel watchdogs cannot have fired (the re-arm at `sendChat`'s read loop runs on the RAW chunk **before** any `JSON.parse`, and the bridge yields `{"type":"hermes_ping"}` on every 20s waiting tick, so `TURN_STALL_MS`=75s needs 3 consecutive missed pings; the bridge has **no gzip/compression middleware** to buffer them — grepped); the bridge's 60s guard only fires when `not got_any`; the 600s guard is 10 min and prints a different message; and the `active_list` probe's status vocabulary was **re-verified against the pin** (`server.py:7397-7407` → `waiting|starting|working|idle`, no drift) and still fails safe (returns True on any error). None of them calls `session.interrupt` anyway. **THE FIX (panel only, `bridge/panel/index.html`; no bridge change, no vendor, ZERO new CSS — 500/500 unchanged):** (a) **`initChat()` returns immediately when `chatBusy`** — re-entering a view is not a session switch and must never touch a live turn; (b) **`selectHermesSession(id)` / `selectSession(id)` no-op when the requested session is the one already open and a turn is running** — re-opening the session you are in cannot mean "kill it" (a switch to a *different* session still stops first, keeping Fable's stop-then-proceed shape); (c) **both lane guards moved ABOVE the stop**, so a call that is about to return can no longer interrupt anything; (d) **`+NEW`, the lane chips and a switch to a different session are unchanged** — those are deliberate gestures and still stop the turn. **DIAGNOSTIC (the brief's 4th ask): a watchdog that fires now SAYS so.** `turnArm(ms, label)` carries a name (`first-byte` / `stall`), every chunk stamps `turn.lastByte`, and on fire the panel prints one console line **and one `{"type":"turn_watchdog", timer, armed_ms, quiet_ms, stage}` INSPECT row** — "watchdog stall fired after 75s · 78s since the last chunk · stage=thinking" — so the next report names its own cause instead of needing this excavation. It also refuses to fire for a superseded turn (`if (curTurn !== t) return;`). **Tests: `test_turn_lifecycle.js` 43 → 52 checks** — six new ones pin the regression fence (same-session no-op in BOTH lanes, both lane guards asserted BEFORE the stop by INDEX, `initChat`'s busy guard asserted to precede both `selectHermesSession(` and `loadSessions(`) and three pin the diagnostic (label + `lastByte` + the INSPECT row + the re-arm asserted to sit before `JSON.parse`). **Two existing assertions were updated HONESTLY** (the escape-hatch grep now anchors `^\s*if (chatBusy) return;` so it still forbids the old blanket no-op while allowing the new *conditional* same-session guard; the two `turnArm(...)` greps gained the new label argument). **All 23 py + 15 js suites green, contract 64, CSS 500/500, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) **the conviction is by elimination, not by a captured log** — the snapshot's `data/logs` is not reachable from the sandbox, so which exact gesture Debi made is unproven; what IS proven is that only `/api/hermes/stop` can produce that upstream text, that only those five call sites reach it, and that four of them changed on 2026-08-14; (b) same-session re-open now does nothing at all while busy (it previously re-loaded the transcript — during a live turn that was always destructive, but it IS a behaviour change); (c) `initChat`'s blanket busy-return also skips `refreshBrowse()`/`loadChatModels()` on that pass (they re-run on the next entry); (d) the "stop then proceed" mis-click hazard Fable already flagged is NARROWED, not removed — a genuine switch to another session still ends the turn. **HONEST LIMIT: not run-verified** — a six-minute Hermes prefill cannot be reproduced in the sandbox. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → HERMES lane → send **"test"** → **let it run, however slow, and click around while it does** (leave the Chat view and come back, click the active rail row, open Mission Control) → **the turn must still complete on its own**, no "Operation interrupted.". Then send another and click **Stop** → it ends within ~3s with `interrupted (forced) · <stage>` and a feed line. Then send another and click a **different** session in the rail → the turn stops and that session opens (deliberate).


> **🧯 THE "STUCK TURN" WEDGE — ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED (2026-08-14, Opus-5 builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA):** Debi's Hermes-lane turn on the local 4B hung in prefill (`thinking…` + the bridge's 20s *"hermes is working — large prompt prefill…"* note), **Stop appeared not to work, `＋ New` did nothing at all, and the AGENT/CHAT chips would not detach** — the panel needed an app restart. **ROOT CAUSE: the panel did not OWN the end of its own turn.** `chatBusy` was cleared in exactly ONE place — `sendChat`'s `finally` — which only runs when `reader.read()` returns or throws. A relay that neither answers nor closes therefore pinned `chatBusy` TRUE **forever**, and every escape hatch was written as a silent early return: `newSession()` `if (chatBusy) return;` (that IS the dead ＋NEW), `selectSession()` / `selectHermesSession()` / `duplicateSession()` the same, and — the sharpest one — **`setMode()` flipped `chatMode` to `'agent'`/`'chat'` while the Hermes stream was still running, after which `sendChat`'s busy branch (`if (chatMode === 'hermes') hermesStop()`) no longer offered Stop AT ALL: the button still READ "Stop" and did nothing.** That is the dead end. `hermesStop()` itself only fired the interrupt RPC and then *hoped* — its own comment said "the stream teardown restores the button" — and it early-returned on `!hermesSid`, so in the **pre-first-event phase Stop was a guaranteed no-op**. The bridge's watchdogs were all working as designed and none of them could help: the 60s first-event guard only fires when `not got_any` (Debi's turn HAD events), the `active_list` probe **returns True on any doubt** (correct — a probe misread must never kill a live stream) and the model genuinely WAS "working", so the only remaining bound was the **600s hard guard** — ten minutes, i.e. "stuck forever" from a human's seat. **FIX — the panel now owns the turn.** Every turn gets `curTurn = {lane, stage, ctl: AbortController, holder, ended, done, timer}`; `forceEndTurn(reason)` aborts the fetch (**which is what unblocks the reader, so the EXISTING `finally` does the real cleanup**), stamps `interrupted (forced) · <stage>` on the existing `.statusline`, and schedules `turnHardRelease()` 400ms later as belt-and-braces in case even the abort is ignored. `stopTurnNow(reason)` is the ONE path out, reached by **Stop, ＋NEW, a lane switch and a session click** — it fires the gateway interrupt **without awaiting it** (`session.interrupt` can itself stall for its own 10s RPC timeout) and force-ends locally regardless. `hermesStop()` now arms the 3s client force-end **before** the `!hermesSid` guard, so Stop wins in the pre-first-event phase too; and the busy branch routes on **`curTurn.lane`**, not the current chip, so a lane switch can never make Stop unreachable. **＋NEW and the lane chips STOP the turn and proceed** (Fable's preferred shape), with an activity-feed line naming the stage it died in. **PANEL WATCHDOG + BRIDGE HEARTBEAT:** the relay now yields a tiny `{"type":"hermes_ping"}` on **every** waiting tick — including while an approval card is pending, which can legitimately wait minutes — and the panel arms `TURN_FIRSTBYTE_MS = 90000` at send and re-arms `TURN_STALL_MS = 75000` on **every received chunk**, so "the model is slow" is now distinguishable from "the relay is gone" without waiting out the 600s guard. The ping is `continue`d before the inspect log so it cannot flood it. ⚠️ **the watchdog is HERMES-LANE ONLY** — that lane has the heartbeat; the direct/agent lanes emit nothing during a long local prefill and arming there could kill a healthy turn (they get the escape hatches, which is the actual fix for deadness). **DIAGNOSTIC:** each turn labels its stage (`connecting` → `waiting for the first frame` → `prefill (bridge says hermes is working)` → `thinking`/`answering`/`running a tool`/`awaiting approval` → `done`) and a force-end prints it to the console AND the feed — the absence of exactly this is why the report could not be diagnosed from the outside. **NOT CAUSED BY A RECENT SLICE** — conv-mode's `turn_start`/`turn_end` hooks, auto-grow and split-view all sit inside the same try/finally and were audited clean; the `if (chatBusy) return` guards have been there since the Phase-3 rail fix, and Hermes is still at **v2026.7.30** (the v2026.8.13 bump is a pending runbook step and is NOT implicated). **A completed turn can never be force-ended** (`turn.done` is set at `[DONE]`), so a ＋NEW clicked in the sliver between `[DONE]` and the socket closing cannot stamp a finished reply. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) all three timeouts are BUILDER numbers (90s/75s/3s; 90 > the bridge's own 60s first-event error on purpose, so the bridge's clearer message wins whenever the relay is alive); (b) stop-then-proceed on ＋NEW / lane switch / session click rather than a "press Stop first" refusal — the brief preferred it, but it does mean a mis-click now ends a running turn (the feed says so); (c) `setMode` became `async` (all four call sites are fire-and-forget `onclick`s); (d) the hermes-lane-only watchdog asymmetry; (e) `turnHardRelease` duplicates three lines of the `finally`'s composer reset rather than sharing a helper — deliberate, because the `finally` also does holder-specific work (`expireApprovals`, `stampLiveTurn`, `convTurnEnd`) that must NOT run twice. **Files: `bridge/panel/index.html` (turn-lifecycle block + `sendChat` + `setMode` + the four session fns), `bridge/app.py` (`hermes_chat` heartbeat, ~L4225).** **Tests: new `bridge/tests/test_turn_lifecycle.js` — 43 checks** (force-end idempotence incl. the completed-turn rule, the abort, the hard release + its wrong-turn guard, Stop-armed-before-the-sid-guard by INDEX not just presence, the busy branch keying on the turn's lane, all four escape hatches asserted to reach `stopTurnNow` **and** to no longer contain `if (chatBusy) return;`, the lane-switch ordering before `chatMode = m`, the finally's timer+handle clear, the watchdog re-arm on every chunk, the constants read OUT of the panel and range-checked against the bridge's own guards, the bridge heartbeat + its position before the approval-pending `continue`, the panel's silent consumption of it, and four negatives pinning that the 600s guard, the 60s guard and the fail-safe probe were NOT touched). **All 23 py + 15 js suites green, contract 64, CSS 500/500 with ZERO new rules, ast OK, both panel JS blocks `node --check` clean.** **HONEST LIMIT: the wedge itself cannot be reproduced in the sandbox** (it needs a real hung gateway), so the fix is reasoned + wiring-tested, not run-verified; and the fix does not make the *turn* complete — it makes the *panel* recoverable, which is the part we own. **Debi verify:** `./scripts/ship.sh` → Hermes lane → send something heavy → while it is thinking, click **Stop** → within ~3s the reply stamps `interrupted (forced) · <stage>`, the button returns to **Send**, and the feed logs `turn force-ended at <stage>`. Then start another heavy turn and, WITHOUT stopping it, click **＋ New** → the turn is stopped and a new chat opens; repeat and click **AGENT** → the turn is stopped and the lane switches. Finally check a normal turn still completes cleanly (no `interrupted` stamp, no feed line).

The replacement is **strictly stronger**: a regex accepting BOTH import forms **plus a new assertion that the call actually appears inside `resolve_pre_tool_block`'s body** — the original only ever pinned an import line, not the call site that opens the approval card. **Verified to pass at BOTH tags** (run against the v2026.8.13 blob read straight out of the fetched submodule objects, and against the currently-checked-out v2026.7.30). ⚠️ **NEW OPS GAP FOUND WHILE SCOPING THE SNAPSHOT SIDE, and it is the reason this pass needs a runbook rather than a script: `ship.sh` deliberately never copies `vendor/`, and the FAT snapshot carries its OWN `vendor/hermes` SOURCE COPY (staged by `build_app.sh --fat`'s rsync) with `.git` STRIPPED — so `install_component.sh hermes` run from the snapshot fails its own `vendor/hermes/.git` precheck, and a vendored pin-bump has NO sanctioned path into the fat app short of a full `--fat` rebuild + re-provision.** The snapshot's hermes venv is an **editable** install of that source dir, so replacing the source in place is most of the bump; the runbook therefore gives an `rsync --delete` (repo → snapshot, `.git`/`node_modules` excluded, AFTER the repo-side web_dist build so the prebuilt SPA travels with it) followed by an **ONLINE** `python -m pip install -e "<snapshot>/vendor/hermes[all]"` — online because 2565 commits of dep churn will not resolve against the pinned wheelhouse with `--no-index`. ⚠️ **PENDING FABLE QA: that rsync-into-the-snapshot is a NEW procedure, not an existing one.** The recorded alternative is the heavy-but-sanctioned `./scripts/build_app.sh --fat` rebuild; both are in the runbook and the choice is Fable's. **PASS 3 (llama.cpp b10295 → b10427): `runner.llamacpp_pin` bumped**, with every flag the sweep verified (`--alias`, `--repeat-penalty`, `--spec-type` + `DRAFT_MTP`, `llama-tts -m/-mm/-p/-o`, `--tts-lang`, `--tts-speaker-file`, and `-mv` still ABSENT) recorded inline, plus the ⚠️ **new implicit config surface** (`common_params_apply_system_config()` now reads `/etc/llama.cpp/config.ini` and `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/llama.cpp/config.ini` **if they exist**, applied before env and CLI — a no-op on Debi's Mac today, but llama-server's behaviour is no longer determined by our argv alone). `install_llamacpp.sh`'s awk reader returns `b10427` against the edited file. ⚠️ **`install_llamacpp.sh` OVERWRITES `data/llamacpp` and keeps NO backup of its own** — the runbook's first line for this pass is the `cp -R` that IS the rollback. **⚠️ THE PIN-READING GOTCHA THAT SHAPES ALL THREE RUNBOOKS: `ship.sh`'s manifest merge is ADDITIVE-ONLY, so a pin whose key ALREADY EXISTS in the snapshot's harness.yaml is NEVER updated by shipping.** `install_mlx.sh` and `install_llamacpp.sh` read the pin from *their own* directory's harness.yaml, so running them from the snapshot after `ship.sh` would cheerfully reinstall the OLD versions and look like a successful bump. The runbook therefore edits the snapshot's harness.yaml pin lines with three line-scoped `sed`s BEFORE running either installer (install_mlx.sh also honours `MLX_VLM_PIN`/`MLX_AUDIO_PIN` env overrides, given as belt-and-braces; `install_llamacpp.sh` has no such override, so the sed is mandatory there). **TWO STANDING FIXES FROM THE SWEEP, BOTH LANDED:** **(a)** the Odysseus branch lie is corrected in BOTH places — `harness.yaml`'s pin now carries an explicit BRANCH FACT block (`dev@25c9e73`; `merge-base --is-ancestor 25c9e73 origin/main` → NO, `origin/dev` → YES; `origin/main` cf4e240 is a stale side branch sitting BEHIND us containing a change upstream later reverted) and CLAUDE.md's "contract check of main@25c9e73" is now "dev@25c9e73" with the same correction. **The pin itself was NOT touched.** **(b)** new `test_bridge_never_pages_history` in `test_odysseus_msg_contract.py` asserts the invariant **on the side we control**: every `_ody_req` call to `/api/history/{sid}` in `bridge/app.py` (there are four) carries no `limit`, `offset`, `params` or `?`, and the test fails loudly if it can no longer FIND such a call (so it cannot pass vacuously after a refactor). The three passes are deliberately SEPARATE so a `[voice] render` regression or a dead Hermes lane has exactly one candidate cause. **Debi runbook: in the session report, in order, with a verify + a rollback per pass.**
