RECON RE-CONFIRMED at pin v2026.7.30 (every July claim holds): `pre_tool_call` in VALID_HOOKS (hermes_cli/plugins.py:136), directive vocabulary `{"action":"block"|"approve","message","rule_key"}` (:2130-2192), `resolve_pre_tool_block` → `tools.approval.request_tool_approval` (:2247-2295, approval.py:3132 — same gate as dangerous shell, `plugin_rule:<key>` allowlist namespace), enforced on the dashboard tool path (model_tools.py:1246-1258, `discover_plugins()` at :214-215), user plugins from `~/.hermes/plugins/` (:1368) gated by `plugins.enabled` (:243-270, opt-in: MISSING key = nothing loads), bundled reference shape `plugins/security-guidance/__init__.py:257` (`ctx.register_hook("pre_tool_call", …)`). **BUILT:** `guards/harness-path-guard/` (plugin.yaml + `__init__.py` w/ PURE matcher + policy.yaml + README) — deny (`~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/Library/Keychains`, `~/.hermes/config.yaml`) hard-blocks, allow (`{HERMES_CWD}`, `~/.hermes`, `{HARNESS_ROOT}/data`, `/tmp`, `{TMPDIR}`) proceeds, anything else escalates → our EXISTING Phase-2 approval card, zero panel work; containment via `os.path.commonpath` (never prefixes — the `/Users/debikEvil` trap), fail-CLOSED on unresolved V4A patches / unreadable policy / any exception. `start_component.sh` hermes branch seeds the dir to `~/.hermes/plugins/harness-path-guard/` (copy-if-changed, `{HARNESS_ROOT}` substituted, `__pycache__` cleared) + adds `harness-path-guard` to `plugins.enabled` via a yaml round-trip + atomic write (idempotent; verified twice-run = "up to date"). **AUDIT TIER C:** bridge reads the same policy at first use → `guard_flag` SSE frame + one `[guard]` warning log beside each out-of-allowlist file_card; panel renders a faint `⚠ wrote outside workspace: <path>` line on the EXISTING statusline tokens (no new CSS, deduped per turn). Also: the approval frame now forwards `description` — a plugin-escalated approval sets `command` to the synthetic label `<write_file> (plugin approval rule)` and puts the PATH in description, so the card would otherwise not show what is being written. > **🏗️ STANDING OPS RULE — FAT APP SERVES A SNAPSHOT (cost us a full session, 2026-07-31):** the FAT app (`build_app.sh --fat`, `fatBuild=true`) serves bridge+panel from the PROVISIONED SNAPSHOT at `~/Library/Application Support/Harness`, NOT the repo. A `--fat` rebuild refreshes the bundle SEED but NEVER the already-provisioned snapshot (only re-extracts if that dir is absent). So after ANY code change the snapshot must be refreshed or the app serves stale code: fast = `cp -R repo/bridge/panel "$DST/bridge/panel"` (code-only, instant; `DST=~/Library/Application Support/Harness`); clean = `rm -rf "$DST"` then relaunch (full re-provision, slow). The LEAN/dev app (`build_app.sh` no flag, `fatBuild=false`) serves the repo LIVE — use it for iteration. Verify which is served with `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8700/ | grep -c <marker>` ON THE MAC (the sandbox curl can't reach the Mac). Symptom of the trap: repo has code X, app shows old behavior, because it's serving the snapshot.

> **🛡️ PORT-KILL INCIDENT — ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED + MAC-VERIFIED (2026-07-31, Fable diagnosed, Opus built):** Starting Odysseus from the panel KILLED THE BRIDGE — `lsof -ti tcp:PORT | xargs kill` matches CLIENT sockets too, and the bridge holds httpx keep-alive connections to :7860/:6767, so start_component.sh's port-clear SIGTERMed the bridge itself (bridge.log: graceful shutdown right after POST start → "Working…" frozen, all Stops dead, BRIDGE UNREACHABLE). FIX: every port-kill is now **listener-scoped** (`-sTCP:LISTEN`) — app.py helper `_port_kill_cmd/_kill_port_listener/_port_listener_pids` (unit-tested 12/12, `bridge/tests/test_port_kill.py`) at runner-stop/eject/switch-cancel/aux; start_component.sh lines 116/151/177/201/282 inline. SECOND BUG same incident: **stale pid files made Stop a silent no-op** (hermes.pid=36254 vs real 41584 — a leftover FAT-root hermes from `~/Library/Application Support/Harness`) — `stop()` rewritten: pid-kill advisory, then ALWAYS verify the port (≤1.5s grace) and kill the listener if still held. Fable QA: approved. Mac-verified: bridge survives Odysseus stop/start; Stop actually stops everything. **Ops rule: any `lsof -ti tcp:` kill MUST carry `-sTCP:LISTEN`.**

> **🛡️ ARTIFACTS 2.1 — nav-escape fix + offline Tailwind SHIPPED (Opus-first BUILDER, 2026-07-31; Mac-verify + Fable QA PENDING, NOT committed):** two bugs from Debi's Tailwind-HTML test. **BUG 1 (security — nav escape):** an artifact's `<a href="#">`/`href="/"` navigated the sandboxed iframe to the harness panel (`:8700/`) because a `srcdoc` doc resolves RELATIVE urls against the PARENT's base (bridge origin). FIX: inject `<base href="about:srcdoc">` as the FIRST `<head>` element of EVERY artifact doc (html/svg/js/react builders) → relative links resolve to a dead base; HTML's first-base-wins makes ours authoritative over any artifact-declared base. CSP `base-uri` DROPPED from both srcdoc CSPs (it would block our `<base>`; unnecessary now — first-base-wins + full sandbox contain it). Defense-in-depth `ART_NAV_GUARD` inline script (capture-phase delegated click listener) preventDefaults any anchor that isn't an in-page `#fragment`. **BUG 2 (offline Tailwind):** model HTML using `<script src="https://cdn.tailwindcss.com">` rendered UNSTYLED offline. FIX: `fetch_vendor_assets.sh` now downloads Tailwind Play CDN 3.4.16 → `bridge/panel/assets/vendor/tailwind.play.js`; new pure `rewriteVendorCdns()` (unit-tested 19/19, `bridge/tests/test_rewrite_cdns.js`) rewrites any `…tailwindcss.com…` script src → `location.origin + /assets/vendor/tailwind.play.js` (ABSOLUTE bridge-origin URL so it loads regardless of the dead base). html/svg frames that contain a rewritten Tailwind tag get widened `script-src` (`'unsafe-eval' <origin>`) so Play CDN's in-browser JIT runs; non-Tailwind html frames keep the tight `'unsafe-inline'`-only CSP. **KEY INTERACTION (got right):** dead `<base>` for links + ABSOLUTE bridge-origin URLs for asset loads — react/babel already used `location.origin + /assets/vendor` (absolute) so the base change does NOT break them; script-src lists `location.origin`. No `bridge/app.py` change (panel derives origin via `location.origin`; `/assets` StaticFiles already serves the new file). Validated: bash -n, node --check both inline blocks, CSS 315/315, rewrite unit 19/19, base-uri absent from live CSP strings. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (1) `base-uri` removed entirely (relies on first-base-wins + sandbox containment) rather than allow-listing `about:srcdoc` — CSP `about:` source support is inconsistent, so removal is the robust choice; (2) Tailwind Play CDN is Tailwind's own "not for production" build — acceptable for local artifact PREVIEW, tagged; (3) React+Tailwind is OUT of scope (react artifacts are JSX source, not an HTML doc we can inject a Tailwind tag into) — only the HTML/SVG path is special-cased; (4) only Tailwind Play is self-hosted; other third-party `<script>`/`<link>` CDNs stay blocked offline (honest limit); (5) elevating html-Tailwind frames to `'unsafe-eval' + <origin>` script-src (contained by opaque origin + connect-src none). **Mac-verify (Debi):** re-run `./scripts/fetch_vendor_assets.sh` (gets tailwind.play.js), restart bridge; ask model for a Tailwind HTML page → Open → renders STYLED; click its nav links → does NOT navigate to the panel. Files: `bridge/panel/index.html`, `scripts/fetch_vendor_assets.sh`, new `bridge/tests/test_rewrite_cdns.js`. NOT committed.

> **🛰️ HERMES LANE — RECON DONE + FABLE DESIGN DECISION (2026-07-31):** v2026.7.30 CHANGED THE PICTURE — the dashboard now ships a full **JSON-RPC WebSocket gateway at `/api/ws`** (session.create / prompt.submit → streamed `message.delta`/`message.complete`, `approval.request`/`approval.respond`, tool.start/complete; built for external clients), plus REST session routers under `web_routers/sessions.py`. **Loopback /api is now TOKEN-GATED** (`_SESSION_TOKEN`; deterministic if we set `HERMES_DASHBOARD_SESSION_TOKEN` at spawn in start_component.sh — same trick Hermes's own desktop shell uses; WS auth = `?token=` query param). **Fable decision: PATH A — bridge⇄/api/ws JSON-RPC adapter re-emitted as SSE to the panel** (full fidelity: deltas/thinking/tools/approvals, sessions; one shared WS multiplexed by session_id). Rejected: per-turn `hermes -z` (no streaming, cold-boot per turn), PTY scraping (VT100 soup). ⚠️ Protocol is upstream-internal — pin-bump contract tests MUST cover method/event names. Decision-free build spec = next Fable task; panel needs an approval-chip UX (new surface, Fable-designed). one command once §G-phase1 verifies: `./scripts/build_app.sh --dmg` → dist/Harness.dmg. Fresh-Mac portability = §G-phase2 (first-run installer) — the dmg alone is NOT yet portable; honesty note in the spec.

> **🛰️ HERMES LANE Phase 1.1 — turn lifecycle robustness SHIPPED (Opus builder, 2026-07-31; Mac-verify + Fable QA PENDING, NOT committed):** fixes Debi's live findings on the Phase-1 lane (turn looked hung during long local prefill; dashboard-side interrupt left the panel spinner stuck until the 10-min guard; no panel stop). RECON (file:line): interrupt RPC = **`session.interrupt` {session_id}** (vendor/hermes/tui_gateway/methods_session.py:2705, returns `{"status":"interrupted"}`; compute-host branch relays via host_supervisor.interrupt, :2717); interrupted turns emit **`message.complete` with `payload.status:"interrupted"`** (server.py:9357-9361→9426; error path status:"error" server.py:7224/1537); liveness probe = **`session.active_list`** rows carry `status` ∈ waiting/starting/working/idle (methods_session.py:726, server.py:7429/7397). BUILT (bridge/app.py + panel): (a) mapper — message.complete status:"interrupted" → in-stream `· interrupted` note + turn end (error variant already ended); (b) **`POST /api/hermes/stop` {session_id}** → nudges the relay queue with a `_stop_requested` sentinel (arms a ~3s force-end fallback) then `session.interrupt`; (c) relay watchdogs — 20s mid-turn silence → panel-visible `hermes_status` note ("large prompt prefill can take a while…") + a best-effort session.active_list probe that ENDS the turn if Hermes says the session is idle/gone (the dashboard-interrupt repro); 60s first-event timeout → clear error; 600s total-silence hard guard kept; (d) panel — **Send becomes "Stop" during a Hermes turn** (click/Enter → hermesStop()), restored in finally; `hermes_status` frames render on the §B statusline. Validated: ast.parse, node --check both blocks, CSS 414/414, test_hermes_sse_map.py extended **23/23**, all other suites green (test_port_kill/test_load_switch "failures" in-sandbox = SOCKS-proxy env only; PASS with proxy env cleared). **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (1) 60s first-event timeout value; (2) Send-becomes-Stop pattern (no other lane has inline stop); (3) session.active_list status vocabulary is upstream-internal — add to pin-bump contract tests with the RPC/event names; (4) probe returns True on any failure (never kills a live stream) — a wedged gateway therefore still rides the 10-min guard; (5) Enter during a Hermes turn now stops rather than queueing. Mac-verify: Hermes turn streams or shows the 20s working-note; panel Stop ends it (note `· interrupted`); interrupting from the Hermes dashboard ends the panel turn within ~20-40s (next probe tick) instead of 10min.

> **🛰️ HERMES LANE Phase 2 — INTERACTIVE APPROVAL CARDS SHIPPED (Opus builder per Fable design, 2026-07-31; Mac-verify + Fable QA PENDING, NOT committed):** replaces Phase-1's auto-deny with a real approve/deny surface. RECON (file:line): `approval.request` payload = `{command (credential-REDACTED upstream via gateway.run._redact_approval_command, #48456), choices, description?, allow_permanent?, smart_denied?}` with choices auto-filled smart_denied→[once,deny] / allow_permanent:false→[once,session,deny] / allow_permanent present→[once,session,always,deny] (tui_gateway/server.py:1592-1611); **NO request id on the wire — approvals are keyed purely by session, resolved FIFO-oldest** (apps/desktop/src/store/prompts.ts:71; tools/approval.py:2198 resolve_gateway_approval); `approval.respond` params = `{session_id, choice, all?}` (methods_prompt.py:864-883); **interrupt-while-pending CONFIRMED auto-denies** (tools/approval.py:3326-3335, #8697 — panel Stop keeps working); upstream approval wait timeout ~300s default (approval.py:3302-3307). BUILT: bridge mapper `approval.request` → SSE `{"type":"approval","request":{command,choices}}` (turn keeps streaming; no auto-deny; missing/malformed choices → conservative `["once","deny"]`); new **`POST /api/hermes/approve` {session_id, choice∈once|session|always|deny}** → `approval.respond` (400 bad input / 502 RPC fail; `resolved:0` surfaced so the panel stamps expired instead of lying); relay tracks `approval_pending` (set on approval.request, cleared by the next event) and while pending SUPPRESSES the 20s working-note AND the active_list probe (600s hard guard stays). Panel: inline `.approval` card in the assistant message (mono small-caps `⚠ APPROVAL REQUIRED`, gold left rail = the ONE accent, command in a pre-style mono block truncated at 400 chars w/ expand/collapse, one chip per payload choice — Once / This session / Always / Deny(faint)); click → buttons disabled instantly → POST → collapses to `✓ approved · <choice>` / `✗ denied`; POST failure re-arms + inline error; turn end with open cards → buttons disabled + `· expired` stamp (`expireApprovals()` in sendChat finally); multiple cards per turn supported. Contract test extended (`test_approval_protocol_contract`): payload["choices"], _redact_approval_command, params choice/all, resolve_gateway_approval, is_interrupted() in the approval wait. Validated: ast.parse, node --check both blocks, CSS 437/437, test_hermes_sse_map.py **27/27**, contract 5/5, all other suites green (proxy env cleared). **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (1) `["once","deny"]` fallback when upstream omits choices (never offer an undeclared persistence scope); (2) card stream-position heuristic — inserted ABOVE `.body` while it's still empty (approval precedes prose), appended after otherwise (true interleaving impossible: body is one textContent stream re-rendered at [DONE]); (3) probe suppressed while pending, not just the note (a probe misread must never kill a card; wedged gateway rides the 600s guard); (4) POST-failure path re-enables buttons + shows inline error (design didn't specify); (5) `resolved:0` → expired stamp rather than success; (6) `all` resolve-all flag deliberately NOT exposed. Files: bridge/app.py, bridge/panel/index.html, bridge/tests/test_hermes_sse_map.py, bridge/contract_tests/test_hermes_ws_contract.py. Mac-verify: Hermes mode → "create a file named test.txt on my Desktop" → card shows the command → Deny → `✗ denied`, turn ends cleanly; repeat → Once → file exists; Stop during a pending card → turn ends, card stamps `· expired`.

## 🔧 MLX ACQUISITION FIX + FAT-INSTALLER SCOPE (2026-07-24, Fable orchestrating, Opus builder)

**Two Debi directives this turn:** (1) MLX model downloads broken — "Failed to load files", Download&Load → "SWITCH FAILED", no progress, state lost on navigation; (2) scope the ENTIRE fat/bundled installer (not slices) + delegate ~95% to Opus.

- **PART 0 — MLX acquisition fix: CODE SHIPPED by Opus builder (Fable line-QA + Mac-verify PENDING).** Root cause: MLX had NO working acquisition path — it rode the runner-switch (rejects "/" HF ids) and the download manager only did per-file GGUF; my own QA fix-slice had earlier removed `_download_progress` (the only MLX progress signal). Fix: **unified acquisition** — `/api/dl/start` now takes MLX repos (empty filename = whole-repo mode: enqueues all safetensors+config+tokenizer via the same Range/pause/resume/parallel machinery into `data/models/<leaf>/`; completion → `_registry_add` a `format:"mlx"` entry, vision iff config has vision_config/image_token_id); panel gives MLX a repo-level **Get** (replaces the "engine-managed" note); `hf_files` MLX branch fixed + non-masking error text; `#downloads-sec` shown in library+browse so progress persists on navigation. Validated: ast.parse, node --check (both script blocks), 21/21 isolated unit test. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** exact "Failed to load files" trigger code-reasoned not reproduced (no sandbox net); MLX file set assumes tokenizer.json/.model (fine for mlx-community); legacy GGUF "Download & Load" primary button still rejects "/" (pre-existing, left). Mac-verify: MLX search → detail lists files+Get → Get shows a live bar that persists across nav → completes → Switch serves locally; GGUF Get unchanged. **NOT committed (no PAT this session).**
- **PART 0.1 — post-Mac-test follow-ups: CODE SHIPPED by Opus builder (Fable QA + Mac-verify PENDING).** After Debi Mac-tested PART 0 (download UI works), 4 issues fixed: (4, critical) completed GGUF download didn't surface in library / no Load — root cause was PANEL-side (backend registry entry was correct; panel didn't flip out of browse view + auto-select on completion) → now flips to LIBRARY, auto-selects the finished model (Load appears), no manual reload; entry factored to pure `_gguf_registry_entry`. (2, Fable design) runner model state machine: new `POST /api/models/eject` CLEARS `runner.model` (was leaving it → Start resurrected the ejected model); state-aware labels via pure `modelAction(isActive,runnerUp,anyLive)` — no model live → all rows **Load**; one live → live=**Eject**, others=**Switch**; runner Start with no active model routes to Models pane to pick instead of assuming. (1, Fable design) Downloads section hoisted above the model list while a download is active, drops to bottom when idle. (3) `_run_download` read buffer 64KB→1MB (no per-chunk flush; shared AsyncClient; Range unchanged) — slow speed reasoned to be HF-CDN throttling not our loop. Validated: ast.parse, bash -n, yaml-parse, node --check both blocks, + GGUF-completion & label-logic unit tests PASS. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (a) MC runner **Stop** still keeps runner.model (pause semantics) vs Models **Eject** clears it — intentional asymmetry, confirm; (b) empty runner.model makes a one-switch Hermes/Odysseus Start fail at the runner step ("pick a model first") not silently break — Hermes already guards, Odysseus seed is `|| true`; (c) Start-routing chose lightweight route-to-pane, no modal; (d) ISSUE 3 throttling conclusion code-reasoned only. **NOT committed (no PAT).**
- **PART 0.2 — load feedback + post-eject hang + live-state desync: CODE SHIPPED by Opus builder (Fable QA + Mac-verify PENDING, NOT committed).** Debi found: no loading feedback on Load/Switch (button stays clickable, no bar); after eject→load-different-model it wedged ("[stream error: TypeError: Load failed]", transient BRIDGE UNREACHABLE, MC green+old-model while Models showed nothing live). Root causes/fixes: (A) Load was ALREADY unified with Switch (both → `_do_switch` bg thread, busy-lock, rollback, 409 on concurrent) — no backend unify needed. (B) **loading feedback**: detail primary button → disabled "Loading…" + indeterminate gold bar + live phase line from switch-status; repeat clicks no-op; failure surfaces inline (no silent wedge). (C) **single source of truth for live** = probe runner `GET :6767/v1/models` — new `_runner_loaded_id`/`_reconcile_live`(unit-tested)/`_live_model_id`; `api_models` returns `live_id`; `/api/status` runner `running = loaded`; MC + Models both key off `live_id` (fixes green-but-not-loaded). (D) probe off-loop via `asyncio.to_thread`; panel declares BRIDGE UNREACHABLE only after 3 consecutive misses (tolerates mid-switch blips). (E) eject→load determinism: port-free-before-rebind already in start_component.sh; busy cleared in `finally` (unit-verified); eject clears switchingId/err. Validated: ast.parse, bash -n, yaml, node --check both blocks, CSS 243/243, new `bridge/tests/test_load_switch.py` 15/15. **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA:** (1) MC runner shows amber "Loading…" ~60-90s during a switch (new visible state, correct per intent) — confirm; (2) MLX live-id falls back to intent (server reports path not our id) — llama.cpp exact via --alias, MLX best-effort, code-reasoned only; (3) failure detection is text-based on the switch log line (no structured status field); (4) ledger still uses port-alive not probe. Validated: bash -n, yaml, ast.parse, Swift brace/paren balance; thin paths confirmed untouched. Build cmd: `./scripts/build_app.sh --fat`. **FAT BUILD SUCCEEDED on Debi's Mac (2026-07-24):** after two build-time fixes (`uv pip download`→bundled-python `pip download`; a `$APP…` glued-ellipsis nounset crash → `${APP}…`), `./scripts/build_app.sh --fat` ran clean: wheelhouse **212 wheels / 283M** (all of bridge+odysseus+searxng+hermes[all]+mlx-lm+mlx-vlm resolved — the big risk CLEARED), seed 112M, ad-hoc signed, **app payload 416M** (NOT 2-4GB — MLX pulls no torch, so far leaner), `dist/Harness.dmg` built. **First offline-provision run (2026-07-24) got past bridge venv (mechanism PROVEN) but Hermes offline install FAILED:** `setuptools<83,>=77.0` not satisfiable — wheelhouse only had setuptools 83.0.0. Debi to rebuild --fat + clean `~/Library/Application Support/Harness` + re-provision. **✅ OFFLINE PROVISION TO GREEN — ACHIEVED (2026-07-24, definition of done MET).** After two more fixes: (a) build_app.sh 4a.1 second `pip download "setuptools<83,>=77.0"` (Hermes build-isolation needed <83; wheelhouse had only 83.0.0) → Hermes then provisioned; (b) firstrun_fat.sh line 63 `$comp…` glued-ellipsis nounset crash → `${comp}…` (same bug class as the build_app.sh `$APP…` one; scanned all scripts, no others). Also learned: macOS relaunches the **/Applications** copy, not dist/ — must `rm -rf /Applications/Harness.app && cp -R dist/Harness.app /Applications/` to test a rebuild. Final: fat app (417M) provisions ALL components offline from bundled wheelhouse (213 wheels) → Mission Control fully green (Bridge/Hermes/Odysseus/SearXNG/Runner all Online, model auto-loaded), Odysseus + Hermes tabs working. **The fat/offline installer is DONE.** Clean `--fat` rebuild done 2026-07-24 → `dist/Harness.dmg` now bakes BOTH fixes (setuptools 82.0.1 + firstrun_fat.sh ellipsis) = the current shareable distributable (213 wheels/284M, app 417M, ad-hoc signed). Switch-cancel endpoint kept (valid for long loads). One unreachable string branch left in `_do_switch` (noted, harmless).
- **Submodule pointer oddity** → **CONFIRMED REAL + FIXED on origin**: committed gitlinks were hermes@9de9c25 (old pin) and odysseus@**168f593 (the broken NameError commit — fresh clones/portable first-run would have bootstrapped broken)**; now hermes=3ef6bbd2 (v2026.7.20), odysseus=e57f60bd. ## 🟢 §G-phase2 — PORTABLE FIRST-RUN SHIPPED (2026-07-23, Opus-first builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA)

Deepest slice so far — bundles a repo seed into the .app + a first-run installer so the .dmg can self-provision on another Mac. Files: `scripts/build_app.sh`, new `scripts/firstrun.sh`, `app/main.swift`, this doc. **Cannot be compiled/run in the sandbox (no swiftc; Linux) — Swift is review-only (braces 76/76, parens 248/248 balanced, all runtime root refs moved to `resolvedRoot`); bash -n passes on both scripts. Debi's rebuild is the real compile check.**
- **`build_app.sh --portable`** — after building the .app, tars the repo into `Harness.app/Contents/Resources/harness-seed.tar.gz` EXCLUDING `.git`, `.gitmodules`, `vendor`, `data`, `dist`, node_modules, __pycache__, *.pyc, .DS_Store. Flag parsing generalized (`--dmg` and/or `--portable`, any order). Portable distributable = `./scripts/build_app.sh --portable --dmg`.
- **`scripts/firstrun.sh`** (new) — runs from the extracted `~/Harness`: checks git (→ `xcode-select --install`), Homebrew (→ brew.sh), Python 3.11+, Node (warn); installs **uv** via astral.sh if missing; sets a **git-identity env fallback** (`GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*`) so bootstrap's initial commit doesn't fail on a fresh Mac with no git config; then `exec bash ./scripts/bootstrap.sh --yes`. bootstrap git-inits + adds the pinned **public-upstream** submodules (hermes/odysseus from harness.yaml repo/pin) + builds the bridge venv — so **no private-repo auth is needed** on the fresh Mac.
- **`app/main.swift`** — new `resolvedRoot` (baked dev `harnessRoot` if `bridge/app.py` exists there, else `~/Harness`); `beginLaunch()` branches: provisioned → normal `ensureBridgeThenLoad`; nothing provisioned + a bundled seed present → `runFirstRun()` (NSAlert consent → extract seed via `/usr/bin/tar` → run firstrun.sh via `bash -lc` login shell so brew/uv PATHs load, output → `~/Harness/data/logs/firstrun.log` → on exit 0, start bridge + load panel where §E guides the rest). `startBridge`/`logPath` now use `resolvedRoot`. **Dev builds are UNCHANGED** — the baked root is valid so the whole first-run path is dormant.
- **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA (§G-phase2):** (1) seed excludes `vendor/`, relying on bootstrap re-adding submodules from public upstreams (viable — upstreams are public; but if an upstream ever goes private/moves, portable breaks). (2) First-run UX is a **static "Setting things up…" screen + `waitUntilExit`** (no live log streaming into the window) — deliberately minimal to keep untested Swift low-risk; live progress = future polish. (3) `bash -lc` login-shell invocation for PATH; single-quoted `~/Harness` path (breaks only if `$HOME` contains a single quote). (4) Homebrew is NOT auto-installed (fail-with-message) — a truly bare Mac needs manual brew first. (5) All Swift is unit-uncompiled here.
- **HONEST LIMITS (tell Debi):** a fresh Mac still needs **Xcode Command Line Tools + Homebrew + internet**; first provision takes several minutes. The portable path **cannot be fully verified without a second Mac / VM / fresh user account** — the baked-root dev build never hits it.
- **Mac-verify for Debi:** (1) **Regression (most important):** `./scripts/build_app.sh` (normal) → relaunch → app opens, bridge starts, all 3 tabs work exactly as before (proves the Swift compiles + dev path unchanged). (2) **Portable build:** `./scripts/build_app.sh --portable --dmg` → succeeds; `tar tzf dist/Harness.app/Contents/Resources/harness-seed.tar.gz | head` shows `./bridge/…`, `./scripts/…`, `./app/…`, `./harness.yaml` and NO `./vendor` / `./.git`; `dist/Harness.dmg` produced. (3) **Full fresh-Mac run:** deferred — needs a second Mac/VM; when available, copy just the .dmg over, open, Continue through setup, expect ~/Harness provisioned + panel + §E checklist. Rollback: normal `build_app.sh` (no --portable) ships the app exactly as today.
- **§G-phase2 VERIFIED on the Mac (Debi, 2026-07-24):** normal `./scripts/build_app.sh` compiled + relaunched cleanly, all 3 tabs work (Swift changes compile, dev path unchanged — the key risk cleared). `--portable --dmg` built: seed **204K**, `dist/Harness.app` 453K, `dist/Harness.dmg` **325K**; `tar tzf` seed showed `./app ./harness.yaml ./bridge ./docs ./policies ./README ./scripts ./skills` and NO `./vendor`/`./.git` — exactly right. Small size is CORRECT (thin Swift shell + text-only seed; vendor/venvs/binaries/models all provision on first-run). **The `.dmg` was BUILT only — NOT opened/mounted/run at all** (Debi, 2026-07-24). The whole first-run flow (NSAlert consent → seed extract → firstrun.sh → bootstrap → panel) is therefore **entirely unexercised end-to-end**; it needs a 2nd Mac / VM / fresh user account (on Debi's own Mac the baked root wins so the app never enters the first-run branch). This is the single biggest untested surface in the project — Fable/Debi should treat §G-phase2's first-run path as "ships-unverified" until a clean-machine run happens. tar-vs-zip: tar.gz chosen for Unix mode/symlink preservation + `/usr/bin/tar` always present (zip would need `ditto` to keep exec bits) — swappable later.
- **§G-phase3 — FUTURE self-contained offline installer (Debi request 2026-07-24, revisit AFTER Fable's current plan; NOT scoped/started):** Debi wants the distributable to optionally **bundle the heavy binaries too** — llama.cpp, MLX, Hermes, Odysseus — so it can install **fully offline**, while still able to go **online to check for updates**. Est. ~1–2 GB (Python venvs would still build from internet). This is a bigger distribution effort (vendoring built binaries + license considerations — Odysseus/Hermes MIT ok, SearXNG AGPL, llama.cpp MIT, MLX MIT; ship-source vs ship-binary per license) + an update-check mechanism honoring the pin+bump discipline. Fable to scope the UX + Opus to build in slices when it comes up. Recorded so it isn't lost.
  - **FABLE ENGINEERING SCOPING (2026-07-24, why the current dmg is 325KB + what a fat build really takes):** the tiny dmg is CORRECT, not a bug — the .app is a Swift/WKWebView shell pointing at the Bridge on :8700; all weight (Hermes/Odysseus/SearXNG venvs, llama.cpp binary, MLX/mlx-vlm venv) is provisioned at runtime, nothing heavy compiled into Swift. A true fat/offline installer is its own milestone with real gotchas: (1) **Python venvs are NOT relocatable** (abs paths baked in) → use `uv`'s standalone Python + relocatable venvs, OR py2app/PyInstaller freeze per component, OR ship a bundled wheelhouse + build venvs offline on first run; (2) **size ~2–4GB** (torch for MLX alone >1GB); (3) **Gatekeeper/notarization** so it opens on another Mac without right-click-override (ad-hoc sign minimum; full notarize needs an Apple Developer acct); (4) licenses fine personal (MIT/Apache; SearXNG AGPL ok personal); model still downloads later (correct). **Decision (Debi 2026-07-24): keep BOTH — the thin dev app (fast iterate/ship/test, proven smooth) AND a fat installer for real distribution.** Sequence the fat build AFTER the Mac-verify sweep + §F; needs a Fable decision-free spec (`build_app.sh --fat`) at greenlight.

## 🟢 ANALYTICS TILES SHIPPED (doc-09 §3.3, 2026-07-24, Opus-first builder; Mac-verify PENDING, ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA)

Cheap local usage analytics in the Mission Control metric strip (Fable ranked this an early Opus task; function only, existing `metric()` primitive). Files: `bridge/app.py`, `bridge/panel/index.html`. Validated: ast.parse, node --check, CSS braces, + an **isolated unit test of the logic** against a temp DB (agent metrics parse w/ nested braces, direct usage capture, aggregation → 238 tok / 2 turns / 66.6 tok/s / 75% cache-hit all correct).
- **Backend:** best-effort per-turn log → **SQLite `data/analytics.db`** (table `turns`: ts, day, lane, model, in_tok, out_tok, cached_tok, tps, ttft). Guarded by a thread lock; **every write/read is wrapped so analytics can NEVER raise into the chat path.** `GET /api/analytics` → `{tokens_today, turns_today, avg_tps (last 20 turns w/ tps>0), cache_hit_pct (cached/in across rows w/ cached>0, or null)}`.
  - **Agent lane** (`/api/ody/chat`): accumulates a 16 KB rolling tail of the raw SSE passthrough (forwarded bytes UNCHANGED — no risk to the panel) and, in `finally`, extracts the last `{"type":"metrics","data":{…}}` frame → logs input/output_tokens, tokens_per_second, time_to_first_token.
  - **Direct lane** (`/api/chat/direct`): added `stream_options.include_usage` so llama-server emits a final usage frame; parse now reads `usage`/`timings` (empty-choices frame handled), measures real TTFT (wall-clock to first delta), logs on `finally`. cached = `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` or `timings.cache_n` (llama-server-version dependent).
- **Panel:** `refresh()` fetches `/api/analytics` and appends tiles **Tokens today** (· N turns), **Speed** (tok/s), and **Cache hit** (%, only when data exists) to the metric strip. Silent-degrade if the endpoint is unavailable.
- **⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA (analytics):** (1) `cached_tokens`/`cache_n` availability is llama-server-build-dependent → the Cache-hit tile may simply not appear (handled: null → hidden). (2) agent metrics `cached_tokens` field may be absent in Odysseus's event (defaults 0) → cache-hit % is mostly direct-lane-driven. (3) tps for direct lane uses `timings.predicted_per_second` (backend-reported); agent lane uses Odysseus's `tokens_per_second`. (4) no retention/rollover — `turns` grows unbounded (tiny rows; add a prune later if needed). (5) metric strip now shows up to 7 tiles (grid auto-fills) — Fable may want to curate which analytics tiles show.
- **Mac-verify (panel+bridge only, NO rebuild):** send a Chat-mode turn + an Agent-mode turn → Mission Control metric strip shows **Tokens today / Speed / (Cache hit)** with non-zero values; numbers grow with more turns. `data/analytics.db` appears (gitignored).

## Doctrine

`agent-kit/DELEGATION-DOCTRINE.md` applies to this project in full, with **one amendment (2026-07-20): all UI design / look-and-feel work is Fable 5 only** — builder subagents may implement UI solely from a Fable-authored, decision-free spec. See `08_Interface_Strategy.md` §Governance.

## 🚨 MODEL IDENTITY PROTOCOL (Debi 2026-07-21 — NEVER violate)

**Background (violation archaeology):** on 2026-07-20/21 an Opus 4.8 session ran this project as if it were the orchestrator — making subtle architectural decisions unilaterally and presenting them as settled — while the chat UI mislabeled it "Fable 5". Debi's verdict: must NEVER happen again. Hence:

1. **Declare your model at the START of every session** — from your actual runtime identity, never from the UI label (the composer can mislabel). State it in the first reply.
2. **Fable 5 is THE ORCHESTRATOR of this project** (per `agent-kit/DELEGATION-DOCTRINE.md` §1): design taste, orchestration, decomposition, root-cause debugging, and **final QA of every diff before it ships**. This covers ENGINEERING, not just UI. The UI amendment (Fable-only look-and-feel) is an addition on top, not a narrowing.
3. **If you are NOT Fable 5** (only **Opus 4.8 high** may substitute, and only when Fable is genuinely unavailable): you are a **BUILDER in temporary Opus-first mode**. You MUST (a) say so explicitly at session start AND **repeat it at every major decision point** — e.g. "⚠️ Opus-first mode (builder): this needs Fable QA"; (b) prefer well-specified mechanical work; (c) **queue genuinely subtle decisions** (architecture, wiring strategy, state machines, security surfaces, anything silent-when-wrong) as *shipped-unverified, pending Fable QA* — never present them as settled; (d) never claim or imply you are Fable.
4. **Every subtle decision made in Opus-first mode gets a `⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA` tag in this file** until Fable reviews it. Fable's review verdicts get recorded (verified / fixed / rejected).
5. If the user addresses "Fable" and you are not Fable, **correct them immediately.**
6. **Delegation-first (Debi 2026-07-23):** Fable orchestrates and DELEGATES by default — builders are **Opus 4.8 only**. Key UI work ships only from a Fable-authored, decision-free spec (no interpretation gaps). Research is delegated to Opus with a Fable brief + Fable coverage-QA. Fable's own hands = the hardest ~20% (token economy). See agent-kit/DELEGATION-DOCTRINE.md amendment 2026-07-23.

- **(a) Own pinned llama-server.** New `scripts/install_llamacpp.sh` reads new harness.yaml key `runner.llamacpp_pin` (set to **`b10094`**), downloads `https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/releases/download/<pin>/llama-<pin>-bin-macos-arm64.tar.gz` into `data/llamacpp/`, extracts to a staging dir, `find`s `llama-server` anywhere inside, and relocates that dir (binary + colocated .dylibs) to **`data/llamacpp/build/bin/llama-server`**; chmod +x; prints `--version` (falls back to the pin). VERIFIED from the release-page HTML in-sandbox: the macOS arm64 asset is a **`.tar.gz`, NOT the `.zip` the handoff drafted** (correction applied); and **assets can lag a freshly-cut tag** — newest tag `b10103` had source-only assets at build time, so the pin is `b10094` (newest tag CONFIRMED to carry the macos-arm64 binary). ⚠️ PENDING FABLE QA: (1) tarball INTERNAL layout could not be verified (release CDN `objects.githubusercontent.com` is blocked in-sandbox) → script is layout-agnostic via find+relocate and fails loudly printing the exact URL + staging contents; (2) pin choice b10094-over-b10103. **First Mac run must confirm `data/llamacpp/build/bin/llama-server --version` works AND that the borrowed .dylibs resolve (rpath) once relocated.**
- **(b) Shared binary-discovery order** (identical in `scripts/start_component.sh` llamacpp branch + `bridge/app.py` aux_start): explicit `runner.binary` → **our pin `data/llamacpp/build/bin/llama-server`** → Jan backends glob → LM Studio backends glob. Error text now points to `scripts/install_llamacpp.sh`.
- **(c) Migrate Jan models + registry "local" source.** New `scripts/migrate_jan_models.sh`: `mv` each `~/Library/Application Support/Jan/data/llamacpp/models/<id>/` (with model.gguf) → `data/models/<id>/` (same volume = instant; skips-not-clobbers an existing target), then re-seeds. `scripts/seed_registry.py` gained `scan_local(data/models/)` → source `"local"` entries (model.gguf + optional mmproj.gguf; download-manager models keep real filenames so they're skipped here and preserved via source `"download"`). **ctx-preservation merge rule:** a fresh local scan has ctx=None (no router preset), so `merge()` carries forward any non-null ctx already in the registry for that id → the 35B's **95536 survives the move**. `merge()` now also treats `"local"` as a re-scanned set (no duplicates on re-seed). Unit-tested against a temp dir (5 cases incl. ctx-preservation, download-preservation, idempotent re-seed, lms `-lms` suffix, empty-jan) — all pass.
- **Mac-verify sequence for Debi (after sync; do in order, stop on first failure):** (1) `./scripts/install_llamacpp.sh` → expect `installed: data/llamacpp/build/bin/llama-server` + a version/pin line, no error. (2) `./scripts/migrate_jan_models.sh` → expect "migrated N model(s)" + a re-seed line; check `data/models.json` still lists the 35B with `"ctx": 95536` and `"source": "local"`. (3) Panel → Runner → Start (adapter `auto`, model = the 35B) → runner comes up on :6767 using the migrated path + our pinned binary. (4) A chat replies. (5) `./scripts/test_hermes.sh` = PASS. (6) Set an aux model + Aux runner → Start → aux serves on :6768. If all green, tell me and 3.1d (jan retirement + Jan uninstall) is unblocked. **Rollback:** `runner.adapter: jan` (jan branch untouched) and/or clear `runner.llamacpp_pin` usage by leaving Jan's backends in place (discovery falls through to them).

**Slices 1+2+3 CODE SHIPPED (2026-07-23, Opus-built to Fable decision-free specs, Fable-QA'd):**
- `adapter: auto` — engine picked PER MODEL from the registry: gguf→llama-server; mlx→mlx_lm.server; mlx+vision→mlx_vlm.server. Port 6767 always → components never notice. Explicit `llamacpp`/`mlx`/`jan` values force an engine; jan branch untouched (rollback).
- Registry `data/models.json` (seed: `scripts/seed_registry.py`): imports Jan's folder (ctx from router.preset.ini, mmproj) AND **LM Studio's `~/.lmstudio/models`** (per-gguf-file entries w/ mmproj sibling detection; MLX dirs via config.json+safetensors, vision via `vision_config`; id collisions suffixed `-lms`). Merge preserves non-import sources (future downloads).
- MLX runtime: `scripts/install_mlx.sh` → `data/mlx-venv` (uv, mlx-lm + mlx-vlm, versions echoed for pinning; auto-installed on first MLX start). MLX servers: loopback, NO api-key, no ctx flag (model config), TRIES=150 (5min load window). Cross-engine port cleanup on every start (switching formats kills the other engine).
- Fable QA fixes/decisions: argv arrays everywhere (space-safe "Application Support" paths — builder draft had unquoted mmproj splitting); approved builder's format-aware registry validation (isdir for mlx, isfile for gguf).
- **Slice-1 Mac verify note:** Debi's first test FAILed only because Runner→Start wasn't run after pkill'ing jan (connection error = nothing on :6767). The `pgrep llama-server` hit was **LM Studio's own server on :52855** (unrelated; useful: newer llama.cpp backend 2.25.2 binary exists at `~/.lmstudio/extensions/backends/...` as an alternative `runner.binary`; LM Studio runs MTP models with `--spec-type draft-mtp` — exotic MTP GGUFs may need such flags, boundary noted).
- **Known boundaries:** MTP-variant GGUFs may not load under our plain argv (use LM Studio fallback or add flags later); MLX tool-calling depends on the model's tokenizer template; Hermes 64K-ctx floor applies to MLX models per their config (no override flag).

## (superseded decision context below)

- **Recommendation (Fable):** switch runner adapter to **llama-server (llama.cpp) direct** for GGUF — determinism (exact file), our own download manager (OpenHuman-blueprint: per-file choice, progress, pause/cancel/parallel), our own registry, ctx control back in harness.yaml, aux runner trivial. Reuse Jan's installed llama-server binary + existing model files (no re-download). Jan stays installed as fallback adapter (`adapter: jan`). Pin+bump the llama.cpp binary like other components.
- **MLX (Opus research, Fable-QA'd 2026-07-23):** MLX = Apple's Apple-Silicon-only framework (MIT). Best server = **Apple's own `mlx_lm.server`** (pip `mlx-lm`): OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions` + `/v1/models`, streaming, HF auto-download (mlx-community org), loads local paths. **Caveats:** NO api-key auth ("not for production") → loopback-only; ctx-length flag unverified → check `mlx_lm.server --help` on Mac; tool-calling template-dependent/unverified; text-only (vision = mlx-vlm later; embeddings unavailable). LM Studio's mlx-engine = MIT *library* (adds structured output + vision) not a server; Jan's MLX = mlx-swift, experimental, weakest. **Verdict: dual adapters — `llamacpp` (GGUF) + `mlx` (mlx_lm.server) — same OpenAI surface, uniform for the gearbox.**
- **FINAL adapter lineup (Debi's in-harness-first principle, 2026-07-23):** primaries live IN the harness — `llamacpp` (GGUF, llama-server binary pinned) + `mlx` (Apple mlx-lm, pip-pinned harness venv). External apps = FALLBACK adapters only: `jan`, `lmstudio`. Debi is MLX-leaning on Mac (faster for their use) → both primaries are first-class co-equals, engine picked per model format.
- **mlx_lm.server TOOL CALLING = VERIFIED at source level** (Opus builder read server.py): accepts `tools`, threads into chat template, parses via tokenizer.tool_parser, emits OpenAI-shape `tool_calls` + finish_reason, streaming incl. — works as-is IF the model's tokenizer has a tool template (else server rejects). Full real flag list captured: --host --port --chat-template --chat-template-args --trust-remote-code --adapter-path --draft-model --num-draft-tokens --temp/top-p/top-k/min-p --max-tokens --decode-concurrency --prompt-concurrency --prefill-step-size --prompt-cache-size --prompt-cache-bytes --pipeline --log-level --allowed-origins. NO --api-key (loopback-only), NO --ctx-size (context from model config).
- **Vision + structured output (why mlx-lm lacks them):** Apple scopes mlx-lm to text; vision = separate `mlx-vlm` (MIT); structured output = sampling-layer (Outlines). LM Studio's mlx-engine = mlx-lm + Outlines + mlx-vlm composed — same compose philosophy as ours. Later add-on slices if needed (vision first, via mlx-vlm — verify its server surface before building); NOT foundation. Tool calling ≠ structured output and already works.
- **What we lose leaving Jan's runner:** silent engine auto-updates (we pin+bump instead), curated quant defaults (we have fit pills), one-process multi-model JIT (we run one-per-port). MLX loss covered by mlx adapter. Est. effort: adapter slice 1 session; download manager 1-2; aux trivial; each Mac-verified, one-line rollback.

## FABLE QA OF THE 2026-07-20/21 OPUS SESSION (done 2026-07-21, by Fable 5)

Cross-checked every subtle decision the Opus session shipped. Verdicts:
- **Model switch (`_do_switch`)** — ❌ HAD BUGS, **fixed by Fable**: (1) exit codes were never checked → a failed model load re-wired components and reported "active: <new>" falsely; now checks each step, reports failures, and **rolls harness.yaml back to the previous model** on load failure. (2) double-switch race (busy flag set inside the thread) → busy now set before spawn.
- **`_hermes_set_mcp` yaml write** — ⚠️ was non-atomic (Opus self-flagged), **fixed by Fable**: temp file + `os.replace`.
- **Panel attribute injection** — ❌ found by Fable, **fixed**: `esc()` doesn't encode quotes but was used inside `href="…"` (source pills, model-card links) → crafted URLs in fetched content could break out. Added `escAttr()`; mdCard link regex now excludes quotes.
- **Verified sound as shipped:** Hermes-config-write approach (vs the hang-prone `hermes mcp add` CLI), single Browse toggle flipping both components (side-effect noted for a later per-component UX), session-mgmt proxy endpoints, HF browser endpoints, dashboard/cron/HERMES_DESKTOP switch, 3-tab Swift shell, Models pane view/switch design (with the fixes above).

## Models pane — confirmed Jan CLI surface + build plan (2026-07-20)

**Jan CLI facts (from official docs, jan.ai/docs/desktop/cli — CLI is v0.7.8+, we have 0.8.3):**
- `jan models list` — list installed models (in the shared Jan data folder; desktop + CLI share it).
- `jan models load <id>` — alias for `jan serve <id>`.
- `jan serve <MODEL_ID>` — MODEL_ID can be a local id OR a **HuggingFace repo id** (e.g. `unsloth/Qwen3.5-9B-GGUF`); **if not downloaded, Jan auto-downloads from HF then serves.** → this IS our download path (no separate `jan pull`). Downloading a new model = `jan serve <hf-repo>` (which also makes it active). We already run `jan serve <model> --port 6767 --api-key … --detach` as the runner.
- Since Jan 0.8.0 llama-server runs in **router mode**; `--ctx-size/--n-gpu-layers/--fit` are **ignored** — per-model context comes from `<data-folder>/llamacpp/router.preset.ini` (desktop-authored). (Matches our M1 finding: preset forced 95536.)
- HF model browser (LM-Studio-style detail): use the HuggingFace public API — `GET https://huggingface.co/api/models?search=<q>&filter=gguf&sort=downloads&limit=N` (list) + `GET /api/models/<repo>` (files/siblings → quant/size) + `…/<repo>/raw/main/README.md` (model card). Fit pill = model file size vs the box's 64 GB. The Bridge runs on the Mac (real internet) so it can call HF; the cowork sandbox CANNOT (network locked) → HF/jan shapes must be spiked on the Mac.

**Build plan (slices, each Mac-verified like M0/M1):**
- **Slice 1 — model view + switch.** Bridge: `GET /api/models` (shell `jan models list` → installed; active = `runner.model` in harness.yaml; running state) and `POST /api/models/switch {id}` (write `runner.model`, restart runner, then RE-FAN-OUT to Hermes+Odysseus — their configs bind the model *name*, so a switch must re-patch `~/.hermes/config.yaml` + re-seed Odysseus, i.e. restart those components too). Models pane replaces the sidebar stub `alert('Gearbox lands in M2')`.
- **Slice 2 — HF browser + download.** Search → results → LM-Studio-style detail (card + quant/size + fit pill) → "Download & Load" = `POST /api/models/switch` with an HF repo id (jan serve auto-downloads). Jan-hub-style fit pills alongside.
- **Slice 3 — Fable visual pass** on the pane.
- **Open question (Debi, either OK):** Models *pane* in Mission Control (default) vs a dedicated *Jan tab*. Leaning pane.

**SPIKE DONE (2026-07-20) — confirmed output shapes:**
- `jan models list` → **JSON array**, fields per model: `id`, `name` (pretty), `size_bytes`, `engine` ("llamacpp"), `embedding` (bool), `capabilities` ([]), `model_path`, `mmproj_path`. (jan 0.8.3.)
- runner `GET :6767/v1/models` → `{data:[{id, status.value:"loaded", architecture.input_modalities:["text","image"], meta.n_ctx, meta.size, …}]}`. **Current model IS vision-capable** (text+image, mmproj present) → image attach viable later.
- HF `GET /api/models?search=…&filter=gguf&sort=downloads&limit=N` → array of `{id (repo), name/modelId, downloads, likes, tags[], pipeline_tag, createdAt}`. (Works from the Mac Bridge; sandbox network is locked so HF/jan can't be tested in cowork — Mac-verify.)

**Concurrent-download answer (Debi asked):** `jan serve <hf-repo>` = download+load+serve ONE active model (no separate `jan pull`). So you can't parallel-download 2-5 that way (port + 64 GB + single-active). Plan: slice-2 download = serial "Download & Load" (switch runner to it). A **parallel download queue** (Bridge fetches GGUFs straight into `~/…/Jan/data/llamacpp/models/<id>/` with progress, no load) is an explicit later enhancement (Debi agreed).

**Slice 2 CODE DONE (Mac-verify pending, 2026-07-21):** HF browser in the Models pane. Bridge `GET /api/models/hf?q=` (HuggingFace search, filter=gguf, sort=downloads) + `GET /api/models/hf/files?repo=` (tree/main → .gguf files + sizes, for fit pills). Panel: "Download a model" search box → results (repo · downloads · likes · pipeline) → per-result **Files & fit** (expand → gguf list with fit pills: Fits <55% / May be slow <90% / Won't fit, vs 64 GB), **Download & Load** (two-step confirm — WKWebView has no `confirm()`; reuses `switchModel(repo)` → jan serve auto-downloads+loads), **Model card ↗** (opens HF via /api/open). `<!-- FABLE: style this -->` — LM-Studio/Jan/Cherry are the reference feel. **Known caveats:** (1) large first-download can exceed the runner's ~90s readiness wait in start_component.sh → status may show a timeout while jan keeps fetching in the background (fix later: longer wait during download, or the parallel download-queue). (2) `jan serve <repo>` lets Jan pick the quant; per-file quant selection is a later refinement.

**Slice 2b CODE DONE (Mac-verify pending, 2026-07-21):** per Debi feedback — (1) **in-app model card**: `GET /api/models/hf/card?repo=` fetches the README (front-matter stripped, 20k cap), rendered in-pane with a light safe markdown renderer (`mdCard`: images dropped, links → openExt); "Model card" button toggles it inline (no more browser bounce), "HF ↗" kept as secondary. (2) **Sort** dropdown: Most downloaded / Most recent (lastModified) / Best match (relevance). (3) **Format** dropdown: GGUF / MLX — `hf_search` passes `filter` + `sort`+`direction`; `hf_files` now handles MLX repos (.safetensors → one summed-size model + fit pill) as well as GGUF (per-file pills). JS validated with `node --check`.

## jan-browser-mcp (step 3) — mechanisms confirmed, endpoint spike pending (2026-07-21)

**Both components take a URL/HTTP MCP → no forking:**
- **Hermes:** MCP servers live in `~/.hermes/config.yaml` under `mcp_servers`. Add via `hermes mcp add <name> --url <endpoint>` (http/sse url) OR `--command npx --args <pkg>` (stdio). (mcp_config.py:426-427 shows both forms.)
- **Odysseus:** `POST /api/mcp/servers` (form) — `transport` = `stdio` | `sse` | `http`; `command` for stdio, `url` for sse/http. `GET /api/mcp/servers`, `DELETE /api/mcp/servers/{id}`, `/servers/{id}/reconnect`, `/tools`. (mcp_routes.py:120-374.)

**TWO different "browser MCP"s — pick before building (transport differs):**
1. **Browser MCP (browsermcp.io)** — Jan's own docs use this; **stdio**: Command `npx`, Args `@browsermcp/mcp`, + Chrome extension `bjfgambnhccakkhmkepdoekmckoijdlc`. → register with command/args in both.
2. **jan-browser-mcp** (Debi's link, ext id `mkciifcjehgnpaigoiaakdgabbpfppal`) — newer; per its listing runs a local **HTTP server on :8181**; you point an MCP endpoint at it. → register with transport=http/sse + url in both.

**SPIKE PENDING (Debi, Mac):** install the chosen extension and report the exact MCP endpoint it exposes (URL + whether SSE or streamable-HTTP for #2, or confirm the npx command for #1). That's the only unknown; everything else is built from the confirmed mechanisms above.

**Build plan once endpoint known:** (a) Bridge helper to register the browser MCP into Hermes (`hermes mcp add` / config.yaml) + Odysseus (`POST /api/mcp/servers`), idempotent; (b) a **Browse toggle** in the Chat pane (agent mode) that signals browser use; (c) the user installs the extension once. Verify: agent-mode chat performs a real browser action with approvals visible.

**Debi decision (2026-07-21): BOTH browser MCPs, BOTH components.** Building in safe slices (each Mac-verified) rather than one untested drop.

**Browse slice 1 CODE DONE (Mac-verify pending, 2026-07-21):** stdio **Browser MCP (browsermcp.io)** registered into **Odysseus** (the chat engine) + a **Browse toggle** in the Chat pane. Bridge: `GET /api/browse/status`, `POST /api/browse/toggle {on}` → registers/removes the `browsermcp` MCP via Odysseus `POST/DELETE /api/mcp/servers` (name=browsermcp, transport=stdio, command=npx, args=["@browsermcp/mcp"]); admin via the existing proxy. Panel: "Browse" chip beside Agent/Chat; on enable, note tells the user to install the Chrome extension + connect a tab; state restored on chat open via status. Panel+bridge → bridge restart, no rebuild. **Needs Node/npx on the Odysseus host (present). MCP shows "connected" only after the extension is installed + a tab connected.** Verify: enable Browse → Odysseus MCP list shows `browsermcp` → install ext → agent-mode chat does a browser action.
**Browse slice 2 CODE DONE (Mac-verify pending, 2026-07-21):** the Browse toggle now covers **BOTH components**. Bridge `browse_toggle` also writes/removes `mcp_servers.browsermcp` in `~/.hermes/config.yaml` via a yaml round-trip (`_hermes_set_mcp`) — NOT the `hermes mcp add` CLI (it has interactive `_confirm`/`input()` prompts + a live-connect attempt that would hang a subprocess; verified in mcp_config.py:434,477,488,606). No connection attempt from our side; Hermes picks it up on **new chats** (config read at use-time). `browse_status` reports `{on, odysseus, hermes, connected}`. yaml round-trip preserves other config keys (model.*, custom_providers) — verified; only comments/order are lost (acceptable for runtime config). **Verify:** toggle Browse → Hermes CONFIG (or `hermes mcp list`) shows browsermcp + Odysseus MCP list shows it → install the Chrome extension → new Hermes chat + agent-mode Odysseus chat can drive the browser.
**Next browse slices:** (3) add the **http jan-browser-mcp** (:8181) once Debi installs it and reports the endpoint — register transport=http/sse+url into both; (4) resolve the single-extension-connection nuance if both components run the browser MCP at once (the extension typically binds one MCP client at a time).

## Research findings feeding the roadmap (2026-07-20, web-verified)

- **Jan model management IS exposeable.** Jan is OpenAI-compatible; the **`jan` CLI (v0.7.8+)** can start the server, **list models, and manage config**; GUI-downloaded models are auto-available to the CLI. Jan's model hub shows fit pills + quant tiers. Caveat to spike before building step 2: whether headless `jan serve` on :6767 exposes download/hub endpoints, or whether **download must go via the `jan` CLI / Jan's management API** (either works, changes plumbing only).
- **jan-browser-mcp** = Chrome extension + tiny local server (:8181), browser automation (click/type/navigate/screenshot/a11y-tree/extract) exposed as MCP. Fits our MCP-native components directly.
- **cate** (github.com/0-AI-UG/cate, MIT): Electron infinite-canvas IDE (editor/terminal/browser/agent panels, git-worktree sidebar, per-chat model memory). Different app class (Electron/React) → **inspiration only**, not a dependency. Borrow: in-app browser panels, worktree UI, per-chat model memory (pairs with the per-session model picker).
- **Odysseus endpoints discovered** (for the proxy, all under `/api`): sessions `GET /api/sessions`, create `POST /api/session`, rename `PATCH /api/session/{id}` (form `name`), delete `POST /api/session/{id}/delete`, **fork/duplicate `POST /api/session/{id}/fork`** (json `{keep_count}` → returns `{id,name,kept}`), history `GET /api/history/{id}`, **models `GET /api/models`** (per-user, cached), per-session model switch via `PATCH /api/session/{id}` (form `model`+`endpoint_id`).

- **One-switch pipeline slice 2 DONE (verified 2026-07-20):** live per-step progress. - **One-switch pipeline slice 3 DONE (verified 2026-07-20):** degraded-state detection. An "expected up" marker (`data/<name>.expected`) is written on successful start, cleared on explicit Stop; `/status` reports `degraded = expected-up AND health failing`. Panel shows red "Degraded" with Restart/Stop + a `health` feed note. Verified: kill runner by port → card flips to Degraded → Restart → starting…→online. State machine now distinguishes Stopped (intentional) from Degraded (crashed).

Optional future refinements, not blocking: install-time closure (flip an *uninstalled* component → install deps too), cross-dependency health cascade (mark Odysseus/Hermes degraded when the runner they depend on dies — currently only the runner's own health is tracked), and the model-download node (folds into M2's model browser).

## M2 slice 2b DONE + design findings (2026-07-20)
- Injected a "Harness skin" `WKUserScript` into the Odysseus web view (`app/main.swift`) overriding `--font-family` (unset → Fira Code mono everywhere) to a sans stack; code blocks keep explicit Fira Code. Colors left to Odysseus's Theme editor. Repo tip `bd61553`.
- **"Premium vs ordinary" diagnosis (Debi's observation; keep for Rung D native panes).** What makes Mission Control / Jan / LM Studio / Cherry feel premium and Odysseus feel ordinary, in 4 levers: (1) **typographic hierarchy** — big serif display title + small mono small-caps labels + body; Odysseus is typographically flat (uniform size/weight). (2) **colour restraint** — one accent (gold) used sparingly on neutral; Odysseus paints coral everywhere (biggest "cheap" tell). (3) **whitespace** — generous breathing room vs Odysseus's dense, border-on-everything layout. (4) **serif+sans pairing** — the editorial signature. **Decision: do NOT chase pixel-parity via fragile injected CSS (tripwire).** True parity = Rung D native panes over Odysseus's API in our aesthetic — a deliberate future milestone.

- **Direction (Debi, 2026-07-20):** don't strip anything from Hermes; the harness's power is "use it as a harness, OR use Hermes from it, OR use Odysseus from it." Hermes must be **interactive with full visibility by default** (see everything as it happens — thinking, tools, approvals), NOT fire-and-watch-only. Fire-and-watch (`hermes -z`) stays as an *option*, added later. Follow how Hermes itself works.
- **The key code finding:** `hermes serve` and `hermes dashboard` boot the **same server** (`web_server.start_server`, default :9119) — `dashboard` is a **superset**: serve's JSON-RPC/WebSocket/PTY API **plus** Hermes's own web UI (embedded chat, live tool feed, approvals, sessions, config). So we don't run two servers; we run the superset and get both surfaces on one port. `hermes -z` is a separate one-shot invocation, untouched (test_hermes.sh still uses it).
- **Therefore slice 1 = Hermes's own dashboard as a third native tab** (Mission Control / Odysseus / **Hermes**) — its real, unstripped interface, so nothing is hidden. Auth: a **loopback bind (127.0.0.1) requires NO password** (`should_require_auth`: host==loopback → False) — unlike Odysseus. Web UI: a Vite/React SPA that **must be built once** — output goes to `vendor/hermes/hermes_cli/web_dist/` (both `node_modules/` and `web_dist/` are gitignored by upstream → building never dirties the submodule / blocks pin-bumps). Build cmd (must run at the hermes **root**, not web/, because `web` is a workspace with a `file:` dep on `apps/shared` = `@hermes/shared`): `npm install --workspace web && npm run build --workspace web`. **VERIFIED building clean in sandbox** (Node 22 / npm 10; 491 pkgs; `✓ built`; web_dist/index.html present).

Pre-launch cleanup hardened (`hermes dashboard --stop` + `pkill -f "hermes (dashboard|serve)"` + `lsof` port-kill). Config patch (runner endpoint) unchanged. If web_dist is missing, dashboard degrades to headless (API only) — never blocks startup.

## M3 slice 1b — "nothing stripped" audit + cron (2026-07-20)

Debi asked (seeing Config toggles do nothing + a theme setting): is anything stripped/removed/non-functioning? **Full code audit of vendored Hermes (Opus subagent, file:line evidence) → nothing is stripped.** `[all]` extras installed; `hermes dashboard` is the complete unstripped SPA (banner: 31 tools · 73 skills). Findings:
- **Config saves DO work** — `PUT /api/config` → `save_config` writes `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (web_server.py:5704; no headless/loopback/CSRF gate). "No response" = Hermes's own **live-apply semantics**: config is read via `load_config()` at use-time, so it applies to **new chats / next read**, never the in-flight turn. Model changes have their own reload path (`/api/model/set`).
- **Most Config sections don't touch the web chat by design.** Web-affecting: model/providers, agent, memory, mcp/toolsets, context. **CLI/TUI-only:** `display.*` (incl. skin), `terminal.*`, voice beeps. **Gateway/messaging-only:** the whole `gateway` block + every platform. Voice/TTS/STT need keys. So Display/Terminal/Voice/Discord toggles legitimately do nothing to the web view.
- **Theme = TWO separate systems.** `display.skin` ("CLI visual theme") themes the **terminal only** — never the web. The web theme is the **bottom-left palette switcher ("HERMES TEAL")**: 8 presets (default/Teal, Midnight, Ember, Mono, Cyberpunk, Rosé, Nous Blue, Teal-Large) in `web/src/themes/presets.ts`, stored in localStorage `hermes-dashboard-theme` + mirrored to `~/.hermes/dashboard-themes/*.yaml`. Already fully working. (Future Fable task: author a custom editorial theme matching Mission Control's cream/gold — clean path via this system.)
- **Gateway/Channels/Pairing/Webhooks/Cron look dead only because the gateway daemon isn't running.** The gateway is a **separate long-lived process** (`hermes gateway run`; also start/stop/status/restart/install). The dashboard's buttons just shell out (`/api/gateway/{start,stop,restart}` spawn `hermes gateway …`) and read its **pidfile** for status. Platforms (telegram, discord, slack, matrix, mattermost, whatsapp, signal, email, teams, +more in plugins/platforms/) each need env-var credentials. **Clean fit as a future Bridge component:** start=`hermes gateway run`, stop=`hermes gateway stop`, health=pidfile. Debi (2026-07-20): don't set messaging up yet — just confirm nothing's missing (it isn't); wire Telegram first when we do.
- **Cron:** Hermes has **no standalone cron daemon** — the ticker lives in the gateway. Debi wants cron working "both" ways → **enabled `HERMES_DESKTOP=1` on the dashboard launch** (start_component.sh) so the dashboard runs its OWN cron ticker (web_server.py:132 `_start_desktop_cron_ticker`, 60s interval). **VERIFIED firing 2026-07-20** ("Custom reminder" job → a session tagged `cron` in Sessions). **Delivery with no messaging channel:** the fired job is delivered as a **new chat session** (the per-platform send path's fallback) — so look in Sessions, not for a push. **Side effects of the flag:** exposes desktop-only `read_terminal`/`close_terminal` tools (inert outside Electron) + minor desktop prompt framing. **No double-fire risk** (earlier note was WRONG): the provider's `cron.scheduler.tick` takes a `cron/.tick.lock` file lock, so the dashboard ticker + a future gateway on the same HERMES_HOME never both fire — whichever grabs the lock wins (web_server.py:141-144 docstring).
- **Submodule mode-flip hygiene (2026-07-20):** an earlier sandbox/bootstrap copy stripped execute bits on ~60 tracked files in `vendor/hermes` (`old mode 100755 → 100644`, ZERO content change) — dirtying the submodule and threatening a future pin-bump (same class as the Odysseus gotcha #8). NOT caused by any chat. Fix on the Mac (sandbox can't unlink mount lockfiles): `rm -f .git/modules/vendor/hermes/index.lock` then `git -C vendor/hermes checkout -- .` (restores modes; discards nothing else). Or ignore mode noise entirely: `git -C vendor/hermes config core.fileMode false`.
- **Inventory — genuinely working now (loopback dashboard):** web chat (→ runner), Config read/save, Sessions, Skills, Models, Keys/Env, MCP config, Theme/Font switcher, **Cron (now, via the flag)**. **Present but inert pending runtime/credentials (NOT missing):** Gateway + Channels/Pairing/Webhooks (need the daemon + platform tokens), Voice/TTS/STT (keys), Memory (provider config). All are installed and in the UI; they light up when their backing process/credential exists.

Release wins: ~80% TTFT cut, dashboard perf wave, security round, durable cron audit, **live subagent transcripts + durable background completions** (relevant to future M5 orchestrator), `hermes update` npm-skip.
- **Mac sequence (isolated ship — do nothing else in the same pass):** sync → `git -C vendor/hermes fetch --tags origin && git -C vendor/hermes checkout v2026.7.20` → reinstall (`./scripts/install_component.sh hermes --yes`, rebuilds venv deps + web_dist) → Start Hermes → verify: dashboard loads on :9119, chat routes to runner, `test_hermes.sh` PASS, cron still fires, Browse toggle still writes mcp_servers. **Rollback:** `git -C vendor/hermes checkout v2026.7.7.2` + reinstall.

## Models pane wave 2 + AUX RUNNER (Fable, 2026-07-23; Mac-verify pending)

- **HF results now show recency** ("27 mins ago" style, `relTimeLong`) in every sort/filter.
- **Eject/Load:** live model row shows **Eject** (stops runner by port → frees ~18GB); ejected-but-pinned model shows **Load**. Chat bar gains a **model select** (installed models, switch = full runner reload + re-fan-out, progress mirrored in the chat note) + **⏏ eject** button.
- **AUX RUNNER (optional, never hardcoded):** `aux:` block in harness.yaml (port 6768, key harness-aux, model EMPTY by default). Models pane: every installed model gets **"Set aux"**; an **Aux runner** control row shows state + Start/Stop. Bridge: `POST /api/aux/set|start|stop` (`jan serve <model> --port 6768 --api-key harness-aux --detach`; stop = kill-by-port). Purpose: point **Odysseus admin settings → Background Tasks** at `http://127.0.0.1:6768/v1` (key `harness-aux`, the aux model name) so aux LLM calls (titles/search-queries/memory) stop hogging the main slot → Agent-mode TTFT improves.
- **LIVE download progress (Fable, 2026-07-23, after Debi called out the placeholder):** switch-status now reports real bytes — `_download_progress` walks Jan's `llamacpp/` tree + `~/.cache/huggingface` for files modified since the switch started → `{dl_bytes, dl_rate, dl_file, dl_phase}`; panel shows a gold progress bar + "X.XX GB downloaded · YY MB/s · <file>" (2s poll), flipping to "loading into memory…" when bytes settle. Total-size/percentage needs knowing which quant Jan picked → still parked with the download manager. **STANDING RULE (Debi): no placeholder UX on user-facing waits — match the Jan/LM-Studio bar or don't ship the surface.**
- **SPIKE RESULT (2026-07-23): hand-placed ggufs are NOT detected** — a correctly-shaped `llamacpp/models/tiny-spike/model.gguf` did not appear in `jan models list` (Jan keeps a registry beyond the folder). → Bridge-side download manager PARKED until Jan's registration mechanism is found; `jan serve <repo>` (now with ~27min wait) stays the download path; quant = Jan's choice for now.
- **App-shell resilience (Fable, 2026-07-23; needs `./scripts/build_app.sh`):** tab webviews loaded ONCE on first select — if the component was down at that moment the tab stayed a white void forever (bit Debi after a model switch: Odysseus tab blank while component showed green). Now: failed loads render a dark "Not reachable yet" placeholder, re-selecting the tab auto-retries, **⌘R = Reload Tab**, dark under-page background. (main.swift: WKNavigationDelegate + failedLoads set.)
- **Fixed 2026-07-23:** `[hidden]{display:none!important}` (author `display:flex` on .hffiles was defeating the hidden attribute — "Hide files" looked dead); **jan supervisor leak** — every runner start left a `jan serve` supervisor alive (port-kill only hits the llama-server child; 8 stale found) → stop/start now also `pkill -f "jan serve.*port[= ]<port>"` (runner, aux, script + bridge).
- **⚠️ Mac-verify risks:** (1) TWO concurrent `jan serve` instances (:6767 + :6768) from one Jan data dir is UNVERIFIED — if the router refuses a second instance, fallback = a bare llama-server or LM Studio as the aux endpoint. (2) Downloading a small aux model via the pane's "Download & Load" SWITCHES the main runner to it — flow: download small model → switch back to the 35B → "Set aux" on the small one. (Direct-download-without-load stays queued as the parallel-queue enhancement.)

## Chat latency — root cause + direct lane (Fable, 2026-07-23; Mac-verify pending)

- **Root cause (log-confirmed):** Odysseus fires auxiliary `llm_call_async` calls (auto-title, memory extraction every 4th msg, web-search query generation) at the SAME single-slot runner as chat turns — odysseus.log shows them taking **5-38s each**, several during Debi's calibration. Turns queue behind them at Odysseus's in-process `_LOCAL_MODEL_LOCK` AND lose the llama.cpp prompt cache. Upstream partially acknowledges this class (their #2927: extraction serialized, but auto-name/extractor default `workload="foreground"`). Remainder of the floor = thinking-model TTFT physics + fixed Odysseus overhead (uncached /slots probe per turn, 3-query doc lookup even in chat mode) — all vendor-owned, not patchable by us.
- **Fix WE own (shipped):** **direct chat lane** — Chat mode now goes panel → Bridge `/api/chat/direct` → runner `/v1/chat/completions` directly (no Odysseus per-turn machinery, no aux-lock queueing). History read from + persisted back to the Odysseus session (`POST /api/session/{sid}/message` {role,content}) so the rail stays coherent; degrades gracefully if Odysseus is down. `<think>` tags / `reasoning_content` split into the pane's thinking protocol (splitter unit-tested). Agent mode unchanged (via Odysseus — tools live there).
- **Config lever (Debi, optional):** Odysseus admin settings → Background Tasks endpoint/model — pointing aux tasks elsewhere (or a small model) would also defuse the collision for AGENT mode turns.
- **Caveat:** direct-lane messages persisted via the slash-command endpoint don't run Odysseus post-turn tasks (no auto-title for those turns — acceptable; our sessions are user-named).

## Rung D slice 1 DONE (verified 2026-07-20) — native Chat pane

- **Mission Control now has a native Chat pane** (sidebar + ⌘K "Open chat"): our editorial UI over Odysseus's API. Bridge-side proxy `/api/ody/*` (CORS default blocks browser-direct from :8700; Bridge logs in as admin via creds in harness.yaml, cookie + re-login on 401). Session find-or-create "Mission Control" bound to `local-jan`; SSE (`data:` lines over chunked POST, `[DONE]` terminator) re-streamed to the panel. Repo tip `951bc94`.
- **Transparency contract (the pattern for ALL future panes):** thinking streams OPEN live in italic serif, auto-collapses when the answer starts, expandable after — and simply absent for non-thinking models; tool lines show `running… → done`; web sources render as concise pills (truncated title + host) behind an expandable chip; links open in the default browser via Bridge `/api/open` (macOS `open`; webview target=_blank is unreliable — WKUIDelegate handler also added for Odysseus-tab links); minimal safe markdown (bold/code) rendered on completion; fixed-height column layout — messages scroll internally, input pinned.
- **Fan-out consequence:** if Odysseus admin password changes, update `components.odysseus.admin_password` in harness.yaml (the proxy logs in with it).

## Rung D slice 2 — Chat pane session management (code done, Mac-verify pending, 2026-07-20)

- **Chat pane now has a session rail** (list / switch / new / rename / delete), left of the chat column. Backed by new Bridge proxy endpoints over Odysseus's session API: `GET /api/ody/sessions` (list → id/name/model/updated_at/message_count, newest first), `POST /api/ody/session/new` (create, bound to `local-jan`), `POST /api/ody/session/{sid}/rename` (→ Odysseus `PATCH /api/session/{sid}` form `name`), `POST /api/ody/session/{sid}/delete` (→ Odysseus `POST /api/session/{sid}/delete`). History via existing `GET /api/ody/history/{sid}`.
- **Panel behavior:** on open, loads the list and restores the last session (`localStorage['harness-chat-sid']`) or the newest; "＋ New" creates+selects; switching loads that session's history; the rail refreshes after each turn so Odysseus's auto-title + recency reorder show up.
- **Inline rename/delete — deliberately NO `prompt()`/`confirm()`:** WKWebView shows neither unless the app implements the WKUIDelegate JS-panel methods (it doesn't), so those would silently no-op in the native tab. Rename swaps the name for an inline `<input>` (Enter save / Esc cancel / blur cancel); delete is a two-step arm (✕ → ✓? for 3s → confirm). Works identically in browser and app. (Existing `alert()` calls elsewhere in the panel DO silently fail in-app — future cleanup or add the WKUIDelegate panels.)
- **Panel-only change** (bridge/app.py + panel/index.html) — no Swift, so **no app rebuild**: sync → restart bridge (or relaunch app, which auto-starts it) → refresh the panel. Not yet Mac-verified.
- **Aesthetic references (Debi, 2026-07-20) for the FABLE visual pass:** likes the chat/session feel of **OpenAI's chat, Odysseus, CherryIn (Cherry Studio), Jan AI**. Per doctrine, look-and-feel = Fable-5 only; this slice ships the *function* in the existing editorial style (restrained, consistent) — a dedicated Fable-authored pass should restyle the rail + chat to that reference feel (decision-free spec → builder implements).
